It always went much better after that, for Cornelli had no inclination whatever to have such a tiresome performance repeated. After such a lesson many days went by before she was lazy again. Today Cornelli had worked quickly and well, for she wanted to have lots of free time before lunch. She had not had time to see the little kid since yesterday. The lesson over, she flew to the stable. Lunch was set for one o’clock, so there was a whole hour left. Matthew spied the approaching child and called to her: “Come here, Cornelli! It is just jumping around.”
===Chinese===
这师生俩是要好的朋友，教师把自己的学生摸得透透的。每逢她精神头足、活泛的时候，他就带着她使劲往前赶，短短工夫能赶出平日三倍的功课；为了不叫彼此扫兴，他掐着钟点一分不差地放她走。可每逢科内莉心不在焉、不肯用功，他就慢条斯理、仔仔细细地往前挪，把她当成脑子多少有点儿不够用的孩子来对待。

他会把这一套一直拖下去，直到钟面上的针超过规定课时一刻钟、半小时、甚至三刻钟。这样一来，午饭前科内莉就只剩下不到一刻钟的工夫，跑去看一眼园子、马厩和鸡舍——那可是她天天都惦记的事。到末了，教师才停下来，拿出他和气到家的腔调说：“今天我不得不拖这么久，因为该做的功课，我们连一半都没做完。你今天领会得有点儿慢哪，科内莉。但愿明天能好一些，不然你的课说不定还得更长。”

打那以后总是好得多，因为这种要命的把戏，科内莉实在不想再来第二回。挨过这么一课之后，总要过上好些天，她才又犯懒。这一天科内莉做得又快又好，因为午饭前她想要一大段自由时光。从昨天起，她还没能去看那只小山羊崽呢。课一结束，她就飞奔到马厩去。午饭定在一点钟，所以还整整剩下一个钟头。马修望见跑来的孩子，招呼她：“到这儿来，科内莉！它正撒欢儿蹦跳呢。”

===Original===
Cornelli ran into the stable, where she saw the snow-white kid, hopping merrily over to its mother and then back again to the hay. It looked so cunning in its gambols that Cornelli went into perfect raptures.

“Oh, you darling little thing!” she called out, patting its spotless fur; “I shall fetch a red ribbon for your neck and then we’ll take a walk together.” The child accordingly ran back to the house, and hunting about among her things, soon returned with a bright red ribbon which she tied about the little kid’s neck. Cornelli was perfectly delighted, for she had never in her life seen a prettier object than the little creature with its snow-white fur and the red ribbon round its neck, skipping lightly about. The next moment it lay down in the hay and looked up happily at Cornelli.

“Can I take it out for a walk, Matthew? Can I harness it to a little wagon and drive around with it?” asked the child. She had many plans in her head, one following on top of the other.
===Chinese===
科内莉跑进马厩，只见那只雪白的小山羊崽正欢蹦乱跳，蹦到妈妈身边，又蹦回干草堆去。它嬉闹起来的模样那么乖巧，喜得科内莉心花怒放。

“哦，你这小宝贝儿！”她拍着它那没一丝杂色的绒毛喊道，“我去给你取根红丝带系在脖子上，然后咱们一块儿去散步。”孩子当即跑回宅子，在自己那一堆东西里翻寻了一气，很快就带着一根鲜红的丝带回来了，把它系在小山羊崽的脖子上。科内莉乐不可支，因为她这辈子还没见过比这更好看的小东西呢——雪白的绒毛，脖子上系着红丝带，轻轻地蹦来跳去。过了一会儿，它卧进干草里，快活地仰起头望着科内莉。

“我能带它出去散步吗，马修？我能给它套上一辆小拖车，赶着它到处跑吗？”孩子问。她脑袋里装着一大堆盘算，一个摞着一个。

===Original===
“Wait, wait; we have to let it grow first,” replied Matthew thoughtfully. “The most important thing for it is to grow, for it is like a baby that has just learned how to walk. It has to stay near its mother and can only run about near her. When it is bigger, it can take walks, and when it is strong and big we can harness it and you can drive it about with two reins in one hand and a long whip in the other.”

Cornelli shouted with joy and patted the kid with new tenderness. She already pictured to herself the lovely drives that they would have together.

“Did you hear the bell in the foundry? I am sure it must be time for dinner. You will have to be a little careful now, Cornelli. Remember that strange ladies are in the house,” said old Matthew with foresight. “You can come again this afternoon.”
===Chinese===
“等等，等等；总得先让它长大。”马修想了想，答道，“眼下顶要紧的是让它长个儿，因为它就像个刚学会走路的小娃娃，得待在妈妈身边，只能围着妈妈近旁转悠。等它大一些，就能出去遛遛了；等它又壮又大，咱们就给它套上车，你一手攥着两根缰绳，一手拿着一根长鞭，赶着它到处跑。”

科内莉欢喜得喊出声来，越发疼爱地抚摸小山羊崽。她已经在心里描画起他们俩将来一同坐车兜风的快活光景了。

“听见铸造厂的铃声了吗？我看准是开饭的时辰了。如今你可得留点儿神，科内莉。记住，家里来了生客。”老马修有先见之明地说，“下午你再过来。”

===Original===
Cornelli had really heard nothing, for she had been absorbed in her new pet. She knew that she ought to appear punctually at her meals, so she left right away. She had also noticed that the ladies were not buried behind big newspapers, like her father. While running to the house, she passed a hydrant. There she remembered that she had to wash her hands, so she held them both under the pipe and rubbed them hard. Then dipping her face in, she rubbed it, too. She had nothing to dry herself with except a very small handkerchief.

“Hurry up! The ladies are already at table,” she heard Esther’s voice urging her from the kitchen window.

Cornelli ran in and saw both ladies already seated at the table. In front of her was a full soup plate.
===Chinese===
其实科内莉什么也没听见，因为她整个儿心思都扑在这个新宠身上了。不过她知道吃饭该准时到场，所以立刻动身回去。她还留意到，那两位女士可不像爸爸，会成天埋在大报纸后头。跑回宅子的路上，她经过一个露天水龙头。到了那儿她才想起得洗洗手，于是把两只手一齐伸到龙头底下，使劲搓。接着又把脸凑上去，也搓了一气。除了一块很小的手绢，她再没有别的什么可拿来擦干。

“快点！女士们已经上桌了。”她听见埃丝特的声音在厨房窗口催她。

科内莉跑进屋，看见两位女士都已落座，她的位子前摆好了一满盘汤。

===Original===
“You have to come punctually to your meals. I am sure that you can hear the loud bell out in the garden,” said the cousin. “But how strange you look! Half wet arms, a soaking apron and damp feet. Have you been in the water, or what have you done?”

“I washed my hands under the water pump and I got splashed,” Cornelli answered.

“Naturally,” remarked Miss Dorner. “There are arrangements in the rooms for washing hands, which involve no splashing. Go, now, and put on another apron. You have to be orderly and neat at mealtimes.”

Cornelli departed.
===Chinese===
“吃饭就得准时来。那铃声那么响，你在园子里也一定听得见的。”表姐说，“可你瞧瞧你这副怪样子！胳膊湿了半截，围裙透湿，脚也是潮的。你是掉进水里了，还是干什么来着？”

“我在水龙头底下洗手来着，溅了一身水。”科内莉答道。

“那可不。”多尔纳小姐说，“屋子里自有专门洗手的地方，一点儿也不会溅着水。去吧，换一条围裙来。吃饭的时候，你必须整整齐齐、干干净净的。”

科内莉走了出去。

===Original===
“The child certainly obeys you﻿—that is something,” said Miss Grideelen. “Since you told her to, she always comes to table properly washed.”

“That is true. But she has the most unheard-of manners,” replied Miss Dorner.

“How shall one get rid of those and start the child on the right path? I must ask you to help her in the morning, Miss Mina. Please comb her hair smoothly and part it the way I told you to.”

“I did it, Miss Dorner, and I do it every morning,” she answered, quite hurt. “Cornelli’s hair is just like bristles and it is very hard to braid. When she jumps it all gets tangled again and she jumps every moment.”
===Chinese===
“这孩子倒真是听你的话——这就很不错了。”格里德伦小姐说，“自打你吩咐过她，她上桌时手脸总是洗得好好的。”

“这倒是真的。可她那副举止做派，真是闻所未闻。”多尔纳小姐答道。

“怎么才能把这些毛病去掉，领孩子走上正道呢？米娜小姐，我得请你每天早晨帮帮她。请把她的头发梳得顺顺的，照我说的那样给她分头路。”

“我梳了呀，多尔纳小姐，而且每天早晨都梳。”她答道，心里挺委屈，“科内莉的头发跟鬃毛似的，可难编辫子了。她一蹦，头发就又全绞成一团，可她一刻不停地蹦。”

===Original===
Cornelli now came back and ate her soup. Her seat was beside her cousin and faced the other lady.

“What is sticking to your dress here?” asked Miss Dorner, looking with disgust at the little skirt. Something was really hanging from the bottom. “Can this be hay or straw? It certainly does not look orderly. I hope you have not come from the stable!”

“Yes, I have,” replied Cornelli.

“How horrid! Indeed, I can even smell it. That is too much!” she exclaimed. “I am sure your father would not let you go there if he knew about it.”
===Chinese===
科内莉这时回来了，喝起她的汤。她的座位挨着表姐，正对着另一位女士。

“你裙子上这儿挂着的是什么呀？”多尔纳小姐问，一脸厌恶地打量着那条小裙子。裙摆底下确实垂着什么东西。“这莫非是干草还是麦秸？瞧着实在不像个样子。我该不会是从马厩里直接过来的吧！”

“就是从马厩来的。”科内莉答道。

“多糟糕！真的，我连味儿都闻见了。这可太不像话了！”她嚷起来，“你爸爸要是知道了，准不会让你上那儿去的。”

===Original===
“Oh, certainly; he goes himself,” Cornelli retorted.

“Do not reply impertinently. In the case of your father it is quite different,” explained Miss Dorner. “I want to tell you something which you must remember. If you are allowed to go to the stable and you enjoy doing it, you can go. But when afterwards you come to your meals, you must first go to your room. Get properly washed there and also change your dress. Be sure not to forget.”

“Yes,” replied Cornelli.

“It is very strange what queer pleasures country children have,” remarked Miss Grideelen. “Have you no books, Cornelli? Don’t you like reading better than wandering around and going to the stable?”
===Chinese===
“哦，那当然不会；他自己还去呢。”科内莉回嘴道。

“不许没规矩地顶嘴。你爸爸那是另一回事。”多尔纳小姐解释道，“我要跟你说一件事，你必须牢牢记住。要是准你去马厩，你也乐意去，那你就去。可过后来吃饭的时候，你必须先回自己屋里，好好洗干净，再换一身衣裳。千万记住，别忘了。”

“嗯。”科内莉答道。

“乡下孩子的兴致可真是古怪。”格里德伦小姐说，“你没有书吗，科内莉？比起到处乱逛、往马厩里跑，你不是更喜欢看书吗？”

===Original===
“Oh no, I don’t like it better, but I have some books,” replied the child.

“What are you going to do in the afternoon, when you have no more lessons to study?” asked Miss Dorner.

“I always go to Martha,” was the reply.

“Who is Martha?” inquired the cousin.
===Chinese===
“哦不，我并不更喜欢看书，不过书我倒是有几本。”孩子答道。

“下午的功课都做完以后，你打算做什么呢？”多尔纳小姐问。

“我总是去玛莎那儿。”得到的回答是这么一句。

“玛莎是谁？”表姐问。

===Original===
“A woman,” said Cornelli.

“I can guess that,” replied the cousin. “But what kind of a woman is she?”

“A good one,” answered Cornelli quickly.

“What an answer!” The cousin turned now to Miss Mina: “Who is this woman? Can the child go to see her? Does anybody here know about her?” she questioned.
===Chinese===
“一个女人。”科内莉说。

“这个我猜得到。”表姐答道，“可她是个什么样的女人呢？”

“是个好人。”科内莉立刻答道。

“这叫什么回答！”表姐说着转向米娜小姐：“这个女人是谁？孩子能去她那儿吗？这儿有人了解她吗？”她连声问道。

===Original===
“Oh yes, she is well known here and was here long before I came,” was Mina’s reply. “She nursed the mistress of this house in her last illness. She is a very good woman and always looks neat and clean. Our master likes her well.”

“Now I have really found out something! You must learn to give proper answers, Cornelli, do you hear?” said the cousin. “You are like a wild hare which does everything in leaps and bounds. You can go to see the woman after finishing your work for your teacher. I am sure you must have some to do for tomorrow.”

Cornelli assented to this, and as soon as the ladies had left the room to retire to their bedrooms for the hottest hours of the day, she sat down at her little table in the corner. Here she wrote down a page with lightning speed, then taking up her book she read her lesson over and over again till she knew it by heart. Soon she was finished, and flinging the books into the drawer, she ran out of the house.
===Chinese===
“哦，了解，她在这儿是人人熟识的，我来之前她就在这儿好久了。”米娜答道，“这家太太最后害病，就是她护理的。她是个很好的女人，一向看着齐整干净。咱们主人挺喜欢她。”

“这会儿总算问出点儿名堂来了！你得学着好好回话，科内莉，听见没有？”表姐说，“你活像只野兔子，做什么事都是连蹦带跳的。给教师做的功课做完以后，你就可以去看她。明天要做的功课，我想你总归是有的吧。”

科内莉答应了。等两位女士回房去歇一天里最热的时辰，她就在墙角自己的小桌前坐下，闪电似的写完了一页，然后拿起书，把功课翻来覆去地念，直到烂熟于心。很快就全办完了，她把书本往抽屉里一扔，跑出了宅子。

===Original===
“Oh, Martha, I wish you knew how terrible it is at home now since Papa has gone,” called Cornelli to her old friend, before she had even reached the top of the stairs. “I just wish Papa was back already and everything was again as before.”

“What is it, Cornelli, what makes you so cross? Come, sit down here a while and tell me about it,” said Martha kindly. She put a chair beside her own at the table where her mending lay neatly sorted out.

“Of course, you can’t understand it, Martha,” Cornelli continued, just as excited as before. “Here with you everything is always the same and nobody comes and orders everything to be changed. Now, I am not allowed to come in any more without getting washed; now, I cannot come out of the stable without changing my clothes. Then I must not wash my hands at the hydrant because I get splashed, and, oh, so many new things have to be done; so different from before.”
===Chinese===
“哦，玛莎，你要知道爸爸走了以后，家里现在有多可怕就好了。”还没爬到楼梯顶，科内莉就朝老朋友喊起来，“我巴不得爸爸已经回来，一切都跟从前一样才好。”

“怎么啦，科内莉，什么事惹你这么气鼓鼓的？来，在这儿坐一会儿，讲给我听听。”玛莎和气地说。她把一把椅子摆到自己身旁的桌子边，桌上她要缝补的活计码得整整齐齐。

“你当然是理解不了的，玛莎。”科内莉接着说，还是先前那股激动的劲儿，“你这儿什么都一成不变，也没有什么人跑来，吩咐把这个那个全都改掉。现在，我不洗手洗脸就不许进屋；现在，我从马厩里出来不换衣裳就不行。还不许我在水龙头底下洗手，因为会溅湿衣裳。唉，还有好多好多新的规矩，全都跟从前不一样了。”

===Original===
“I am sure, Cornelli, that it is not at all bad that things should not always be the way they were before,” said Martha reflectively. “I believe that the lady who is related to you wants the same thing from you that your mother would have wished had she lived. This is very good for you. Of course, Miss Mina and Esther mean well, but your relation knows much better what is to be done to make you grow up the way your mother would have desired. Just think how happy your father would be if you should resemble your mother and he be reminded of her every time he looked at you. You well know what great joy that would be to him.”

Cornelli did know that her father would be very happy then, for he had made many remarks which she had understood. A short time ago he had said that his cousin found no likeness between his child and her mother, and Cornelli had observed the sad expression of his eyes when he had said it.

Cornelli shook her head. “You said once that my mother was different from anybody,” she said. “So I can’t ever be like her; you said so yourself, Martha.”
===Chinese===
“科内莉，依我看，事情不该永远是从前那个样子，这压根儿算不上坏事。”玛莎若有所思地说，“我相信，你那位亲眷期望于你的，正是你母亲倘若还在世，也会期望于你的。这对你大有好处。米娜小姐和埃丝特当然都是好意，可你这位亲眷更懂得，怎样做才能让你长成你母亲指望的模样。你想想，要是你长得像你母亲，你爸爸每回瞧见你，就想起她来，他会多高兴啊。你很清楚，那对他将是天大的喜事。”

科内莉确实知道，到那时爸爸会非常高兴，因为他吐露过许多话，她都听懂了。就在不久前，他说过他的表姐在孩子和她母亲之间找不出一点儿相像的地方；他说这话的时候，眼睛里那副悲伤的神情，科内莉全都看在了眼里。

科内莉摇了摇头。“你从前说过，我母亲跟谁都不一样。”她说，“所以我永远也没法像她；这话是你自己说的，玛莎。”

===Original===
“Yes, yes, I have said that,” confirmed Martha. “But I have to explain something to you, Cornelli. If you can’t become exactly like your mother, you certainly can become more like her than anybody else, for you are her child, and a child always has something from her mother. I have seen you look at me just the way she did, with the same brown eyes; but not when you frown the way you do today. You must try to watch the two ladies very carefully in all they do and in the way they speak. They are your mother’s kind, and that is why I am so glad that you can watch their manners and can try to imitate them. You can learn to resemble your mother in your ways, if you copy the ladies.”

“Yes, I shall do that,” agreed Cornelli. “Just the same, I am not terribly pleased that they are here and that everything has to be changed. Oh dear, I have just remembered that I have to be back now and drink some hot coffee and milk, because Miss Dorner says that the afternoons are so frightfully long in the country they have to be interrupted. At that time I always used to get from the garden some apples or cherries or whatever else there was, and they always tasted so awfully good. If I only could lengthen my afternoon, which seems too long to them! I never can do all I plan to do. Goodbye, Martha.”

And with these words Cornelli ran away.
===Chinese===
“是，是，这话我是说过。”玛莎认了下来，“可有件事我得跟你说清楚，科内莉。就算你不能变得跟你母亲一模一样，你也一定能比任何人都更像她，因为你是她的孩子，孩子身上总带着母亲的影子。我见过你望我的那副神情，跟她当年一模一样，连那双棕色的眼睛都一样——不过可不是在你像今天这样皱眉头的时候。你要留心细看那两位女士的一举一动、一言一语。她们和你母亲是一路人，所以我才这么高兴，高兴你能看她们的举止，照着她们学。只要你肯学她们的样子，你就能在言谈举止上长得像你的母亲。”

“嗯，我会照办的。”科内莉应承下来，“不过话说回来，她们来了、什么都得改，我心里实在不怎么痛快。哎呀，不好，我这才想起来，这会儿就得赶回去，喝热咖啡加牛奶了，因为多尔纳小姐说，乡下的下午长得了不得，非得中间打断一回不可。从前一到这个时辰，我总能从园子里弄到些苹果啊、樱桃啊，或是别的什么果子，吃起来别提多香了。要是能把我那在她们眼里太长的下午拉长些就好了！我想做的事，从来就没有做得完的时候。再见，玛莎。”

说完这句话，科内莉就跑走了。

## IV / 四

The Unwished-for Happens / 不愿发生的事偏偏发生了

===Original===
Esther, the able mistress of the kitchen, was standing in the garden picking green peas, which hung in clusters from the vines. They had ripened quickly in the sunny June weather.

“Come down here, Cornelli!” she called. “Just see how many peas there are! Why do you steal about so quietly nowadays, and why don’t you run the way you used to?”

“I am not allowed to do anything any more,” replied Cornelli, approaching her. “Mina is beginning to tell me that I even must not jump, for it might tangle my hair. I wish I had not a single hair left; then I could at least run and jump about.”

===Chinese===
厨房里那位能干的主妇埃丝特（Esther）正站在园子里摘青豌豆，一串串豆荚从藤蔓上垂挂下来。在六月晴暖的太阳底下，它们熟得很快。

"快到这儿来，科内莉（Cornelli）！"她喊道。"你瞧瞧这豌豆结了有多少！这些日子你怎么老是蹑手蹑脚的，也不像从前那样撒开腿跑了？"

"现在我什么都不许做了。"科内莉朝她走过去，答道。"米娜（Mina）都开始对我说，连蹦跳也不许了，怕把头发跳乱。我真恨不得一根头发都不剩，那样子我至少还能跑一跑、蹦一蹦。"

===Original===
“No, no, child; that would look too dreadful. Just imagine it! But don’t get sad on account of that,” Esther consoled her. “Just jump around as before! Your hair can always be put in order again. Why haven’t you come into the kitchen lately to see if things taste right?”

“I am not allowed to; Miss Dorner says that is bad manners,” Cornelli informed her.

“Oh, I see! Well, you might do worse things. However, you must obey! Yes, you have to obey,” Esther repeated. “Don’t you go to Miss Mina any more, either, when she fixes the dessert?”

Cornelli shook her head.

Miss Mina had quickly understood the new order that had begun in the household and accordingly had suited herself to it. When she thought the ladies would not approve of an old custom, she dropped it quickly, and Cornelli had soon noticed her change of attitude.

===Chinese===
"不不，孩子，那可就难看死了。你自己想想看！不过别为这个难过。"埃丝特安慰她。"还像从前那样蹦蹦跳跳就是了！头发乱了，总还能重新梳好的。这些日子你怎么不上厨房来了？也不来尝尝东西的味道对不对？"

"是不许我来；多尔纳小姐（Miss Dorner）说，那是没有规矩。"科内莉告诉她。

"哦，原来如此！唉，人嘛，本来还可能干出更不像话的事来。不过呢，你得听话！是啊，非听话不可。"埃丝特又重复了一遍。"米娜小姐做甜点的时候，你也不再去凑在一旁了吧？"

科内莉摇了摇头。

米娜小姐很快就摸清了这个家里新开始的规矩，便马上让自己去顺应了它。凡是她料想两位小姐不会赞成的老习惯，她都立刻丢开手，科内莉不多时就看出了她态度的转变。

===Original===
“I don’t care if I never can go to the pantry any more, I don’t care,” Cornelli exploded now. “She can eat all the things herself which drop beside the plate. I don’t care. I don’t want anything as long as I can go to the little kid in the stable; it really is the most cunning creature in the whole world. Have you seen it yet, Esther?”

“Certainly I have, and why not?” the cook replied. “Matthew took me out to the stable as soon as it was born. You can certainly go to see it as long as it is in our own stable. Just go there as much as you like! Nobody can forbid you that.”

“My teacher is coming,” Cornelli now exclaimed, “and I have to go.”

“Yes, child, but do keep up your spirits. There are lots of pleasant things still left for you to enjoy. Just wait till you taste the strawberry tarts I am going to make today.”

With these words Esther smacked her lips to express the great succulence of the promised dish.

===Chinese===
"就是从此再也不许我进配膳室，我也不在乎，我不在乎。"科内莉这会儿爆发了。"掉在盘子旁边的东西，她就自个儿全吃掉好了。我不在乎。只要还能到厩房里去看那只小山羊，我什么都不要。它真是全世界最伶俐可爱的小东西。你见过它了吗，埃丝特？"

"当然见过，干吗不见识见识？"厨娘回答。"它一落生，马修（Matthew）就带我到厩里去看过了。只要它待在咱们自己的厩里，你尽管去看，爱去多少回去多少回！这个谁也禁不了你。"

"我的老师要来了，"科内莉这会儿喊道，"我得走了。"

"去吧，孩子，可要打起精神来。留着给你享受的乐事还多着呢。就等着尝我今天要做的草莓果馅饼吧。"

说这话时，埃丝特咂了咂嘴，好表示那答应下的点心该有多么香甜可口。

===Original===
“I wouldn’t even care if you baked nettle tarts; I wish I didn’t have to eat at table and could just eat berries in the garden and drink milk in the stable.”

Cornelli ran towards the house, for she had forgotten to walk sedately, as she had been told to do.

While Cornelli had her lessons upstairs in the living room, in the jessamine arbor both ladies were sitting on a garden bench.

===Chinese===
"你就是烤荨麻果馅饼我也不在乎。我倒盼着不必坐在桌边吃饭，就在园子里吃浆果，在厩里喝牛奶呢。"

科内莉朝屋子跑去，因为她忘了人家嘱咐过她，走路要稳稳当当的。

科内莉在楼上起居室里上课的当儿，两位小姐正坐在素馨花凉亭里的一条花园长椅上。

===Original===
“It would be so pleasant and agreeable here,” said Miss Dorner, “and my cousin could have such a very charming life, if the child were only a little different. Don’t you think, Betty, that she has no manners whatever?”

“Yes, but she has had no training at all,” remarked Miss Grideelen; “and she may have inherited some qualities from her mother.”

“Oh no, not a single trait! You cannot possibly imagine a greater difference than between the mother and this child,” Miss Dorner exclaimed. “Cornelia was full of amiability and gayety. She always greeted and cheered everyone with her laughing brown eyes. If my cousin could only have the happiness to see his child resemble her mother the slightest bit! He was so fond of his wife! He deserves this joy, for he is a splendid man.”

“It is curious how very different children can be from their parents,” said Miss Grideelen with regret in her voice. “But I am sure that something can still be accomplished by educating the child. Many qualities can be developed that hardly show themselves yet. We ought to do our best for her, especially for her father’s sake.”

“That is just what I am doing, Betty. Unfortunately, I have had very little success as yet,” answered Miss Dorner. “But I just hope that the day will come when I can write her father some pleasant news about Cornelli, something different from what I feel obliged to send him now.”

===Chinese===
"这里本来可以又舒服又惬意，"多尔纳小姐说，"我表哥的日子本来可以过得非常之好，只要这孩子稍微像点样就行了。你说呢，贝蒂（Betty），她不是一点规矩都没有吗？"

"是啊，可她压根儿没受过一点调教，"格里德伦小姐（Miss Grideelen）说，"再说，说不定有些品性是从她母亲那里继承来的。"

"哦不，一丁点儿都不像！母女俩之间会有多大差别，你再也想象不出比这更大的了。"多尔纳小姐喊道。"科内莉亚（Cornelia）生前多么和蔼可亲、爱说爱笑。她那双含笑的棕色眼睛，见了谁都是又打招呼又逗人开心。我表哥要是能有那份福气，看见孩子有半分像她母亲就好了！他多爱他的妻子啊！他受之无愧，他是个那么出色的人。"

"孩子跟父母竟然能差到这个地步，真是奇怪。"格里德伦小姐说，话音里带着惋惜。"不过我确信，好好教育这孩子，总还能有所成就。许多眼下几乎显不出来的品性，都是可以培养出来的。我们应该为她尽心竭力，尤其是看在她父亲的分上。"

"我干的正是这件事呀，贝蒂。可惜直到如今，成效甚微。"多尔纳小姐回答。"不过我还是盼着有那么一天，我能给她的父亲写点关于科内莉的让人高兴的消息，而不是像眼下这样，不得不给他捎去另一种信。"

===Original===
The day had been exceedingly hot, and the ladies retired to their rooms immediately after dinner, while Cornelli, according to her custom, obediently did her lessons. Then she disappeared. In the late evening, when the ladies sat down to supper, it was so warm that Miss Mina was ordered to open all the windows.

Now Cornelli entered.

“For mercy’s sake, what are you thinking of!” the cousin accosted the child. “We are nearly perishing with the heat and you put on a fur dress, which you could wear without a coat in a sleigh ride in the middle of winter. Why do you do such foolish things?”

Cornelli was really attired very strangely. Her little dress was made of such heavy, fur-like material that one could see it was meant for the coldest winter weather, and for someone who disliked much outer clothing. The child’s cheeks were glowing red, and from the insufferable heat whole streams of perspiration trickled down her face.

===Chinese===
这一天热极了，两位小姐一吃过午饭便回自己房里去了；科内莉呢，照着她的老习惯，乖乖地做功课。随后她就不见了踪影。傍晚很晚的时候，两位小姐坐下吃晚饭，天气还是那么热，只好吩咐米娜小姐把所有的窗子都敞开。

这时科内莉进来了。

"老天爷在上，你是怎么想的！"表姐劈头就数落这孩子。"我们热得都快受不住了，你倒好，穿上一身毛皮裙子——这样的裙子，寒冬腊月坐着雪橇兜风，连大衣都不用套也尽够了。你干吗干这种傻事？"

科内莉这身穿戴实在古怪极了。她的小裙子用的是那么厚重的毛皮般的料子，一看便知是给最冷的天气预备的，还是给不爱多穿外衣的人穿的。孩子两颊烧得通红，那难以忍受的闷热里，一道道汗水顺着她的脸往下淌。

===Original===
“I have no more dresses left,” she said stubbornly.

“Can you understand it?” asked the cousin, looking at her friend.

“I really think that this is the fifth dress in which I have seen Cornelli today,” answered the friend. “In the early morning I saw her running across the yard in a dark dress. At breakfast she wore a light frock and for lunch a red one. I believe that she wore a blue dress when we had our coffee this afternoon, so this must be the fifth costume. I was beginning at lunch time to wonder about the frequent changes.”

“I have to change my dress every time I go to the stable,” Cornelli said, a little more stubbornly than before.

===Chinese===
"我没有裙子了。"她倔头倔脑地说。

"你能理解吗？"表姐望着自己的朋友问。

"我数着，这已经是我今天看见科内莉穿的第五条裙子了。"朋友回答。"一大早，我看见她穿一条深色的裙子跑过院子。吃早饭时她换了件浅色的，午饭又是一条红的。下午我们喝咖啡的当儿，我记得她穿的是蓝的，那么这一身准是第五套了。从午饭时候起，我就在纳闷她怎么换得这么勤。"

"我每去一趟厩房，就得换一条裙子。"科内莉说，比先前更犟了。

===Original===
“How can anybody be so foolish!” exclaimed the cousin now. “I can understand now why you have no fun and why you always wear an unhappy face. You must be nearly perished with the heat! Finish your supper quickly and then go to your room and take off this heavy dress. You surely have another dress. I must forbid you to go to the stable from now on! You can see for yourself what comes of it! If only you would not frown like this, Cornelli. You look exactly as if you had two little horns growing on your forehead, one on each side. There are many other and better amusements for you than spending your life in the stable. Are you able to embroider?”

“No,” Cornelli answered curtly.

“Children of your age ought to be able to, though,” said the cousin. “But we have not come here to teach you that; have we, Betty? You probably do not even know how to hold the needle in your hand.”

“Why should it be necessary for Cornelli to learn embroidery just now?” replied the friend. “She has lovely books that she can read; she has shown us some herself. Don’t you prefer reading a pretty story to running about in the stable, child?”

“No, I don’t,” replied Cornelli crossly.

===Chinese===
"怎么会有这么傻的人！"表姐这会儿喊了起来。"我现在可明白了，你为什么老不痛快，为什么总绷着一张不高兴的脸。这会儿你准是热坏了吧！快把晚饭吃了，然后回你自己屋里，把这身厚裙子脱了。你总还有别的裙子吧。从今往后，我禁止你再去厩房！你自己瞧瞧，这都是招的什么事！别这么皱着眉头行不行，科内莉。你这模样，活像脑门上长了两只小犄角，一边一只。比把日子泡在厩房里好得多、有意思得多的玩意儿有的是。你会刺绣吗？"

"不会。"科内莉没好气地答道。

"不过，你这个年纪的孩子本该会了。"表姐说。"可我们不是来教你这个的，对吧，贝蒂？你八成连针都还不会拿吧。"

"科内莉这会儿干吗非得学刺绣呢？"朋友回答。"她有好些可爱的书可以读，有几本还是她亲自拿给我们看的。跟在厩房里跑来跑去比起来，你不更愿意读一个好听的故事吗，孩子？"

"不，我不愿意。"科内莉气冲冲地回答。

===Original===
“We must not pay attention to what she says,” remarked Miss Dorner. “When Cornelli is bored, she will probably turn to her books herself. Please, Miss Mina, keep an eye on Cornelli. Nonsense like this must not happen any more.”

When supper was finished, Cornelli went up to her room, and Miss Mina followed her.

“You certainly don’t need to do such silly things,” she said scoldingly, as soon as they were on the stairs, where her words could not be overheard. “I have enough to do nowadays without watching whether you put on a new dress every few hours.”

“It isn’t my fault,” Cornelli replied morosely. “They ordered me to do it.”

“They won’t always smell it when you have been to the stable,” scolded Miss Mina.

===Chinese===
"她说的话，我们不必放在心上。"多尔纳小姐说。"等科内莉闲得发慌了，自会去翻她的书的。米娜小姐，请你盯着科内莉点儿。这样的荒唐事，往后不许再发生。"

晚饭吃完，科内莉上楼回自己的房间去，米娜小姐跟了上来。

"你实在犯不上干这种蠢事。"一走到话头传不到别人耳朵里的楼梯上，她就数落开了。"如今我的事就够多的了，还得再盯着你是不是每隔几个钟头换一条新裙子。"

"这不赖我。"科内莉闷闷地回嘴。"是她们吩咐的。"

"你去过厩房，她们也不是回回都闻得出来。"米娜小姐责备道。

===Original===
“Yes, but they do smell it,” Cornelli retorted, “and even if they didn’t, I should have to obey. They told me to change every time I go to the stable.”

“Yes, but now you are told not to go there any more, remember that!﻿—so your frequent changing will have to stop,” grumbled Miss Mina, while she was helping Cornelli to take off her hot dress.

“Now I have to clean it, besides! You actually give more work than six well brought up children.” Miss Mina had never before spoken so roughly to Cornelli, for she had always been anxious to keep in the child’s good graces. But she had suddenly ceased to care about that.

Cornelli looked at her with astonishment. The child’s eyes were also full of something that nobody had ever seen there before. Mina seemed to understand: “I did not do you any harm,” she said quickly; “what I have said is only the truth.” With that she left the room.

“If everybody treats me that way I’ll be that way, too,” cried Cornelli with a furious look. Suddenly taking hold of the dress she had just taken off she threw it out of the window. After a while Mina returned, bringing back the dress. Cornelli was sitting on the windowsill crossly looking down at the yard.

===Chinese===
"是闻得出来，她们就是闻得出来。"科内莉顶了回去，"就算闻不出来，我也得听话呀。她们吩咐过我，每去一趟厩房就得换裙子。"

"是啊，可如今人家又发话不许你再去了，给我记住了！——这么着，你这套勤换衣裳的把戏也该收场了。"米娜小姐一面咕哝，一面帮着科内莉脱下那身热得要命的裙子。

"这下还得我来收拾它！你可真是比六个有教养的孩子加在一起还费事。"米娜小姐从前从来没对科内莉说得这么粗声大气，因为她一向巴望着讨这孩子的欢心。可她忽然就满不在乎了。

科内莉惊讶地望着她。孩子的眼睛里还盛满了一种谁都没在那儿见过的东西。米娜像是看懂了。"我没伤害你的意思，"她赶忙说，"我说的不过是实话。"说完她就出了房门。

"要是人人都这么待我，我也就这么待人！"科内莉怒气冲冲地喊道。她忽然抓起刚脱下来的那条裙子，一把扔出了窗外。过了一会儿，米娜回来了，把裙子捡了回来。科内莉正坐在窗台上，气鼓鼓地朝下瞪着院子。

===Original===
“Look out that the wind doesn’t blow you down, too, like your dress,” Miss Mina said unpleasantly.

“I don’t care,” Cornelli replied obstinately. “It did not blow down at all, for I threw it down on purpose.”

“Oh, is this the way you behave? Next time you can get it yourself,” said Miss Mina, running away indignantly.

Next morning Cornelli was walking across the courtyard, happily talking to her teacher, whose hand she was holding. During her school hours she had forgotten all the troubles of the day before, for Mr. Malinger had been as kind to her as ever. He at least had not changed.

“Could you give me a little rose?” he asked smilingly, while they were passing the blooming rose bushes. So Cornelli quickly ran from bush to bush till she had gathered a fine bunch of dark and light, white and red roses. These she offered to her teacher, warning him not to prick himself. Then the two parted most cordially.

===Chinese===
"留神别让风也把你刮下去，跟你那裙子一样。"米娜小姐没好气地说。

"我才不在乎呢。"科内莉执拗地回答。"它根本不是让风刮下去的，是我故意扔下去的。"

"哦，你就是这样行事的？下回你自己去捡吧。"米娜小姐说着，气冲冲地跑掉了。

第二天早晨，科内莉牵着老师的手走过院子，快快活活地跟他说着话。上课的几个钟头里，她把前一天的种种烦恼全忘光了，因为梅林格先生（Mr. Malinger）待她还像往常一样和气。至少他没有变。

"能送我一朵小小的玫瑰吗？"走过开得正盛的玫瑰丛时，他笑吟吟地问。科内莉便飞快地从一丛跑到另一丛，直到采成了一大把好玫瑰，深色的浅色的、白的红的都有。她把花献给老师，还叮嘱他小心扎手。随后两人亲亲热热地道了别。

===Original===
Cornelli, on coming back, ran swiftly toward the stable. Suddenly, however, she stood stock still, for she remembered that she was not allowed to go there any more. No longer could she see the darling little kid and watch its growth. She would be unable to tell when the moment had come for it to be hitched to a carriage to be driven about by her. She might not be allowed even to do that! She hoped, however, that her father might be back by that time and that then everything would be different. Cornelli danced with joy at that thought, and her old gaiety seemed to return. She felt like going to Esther and talking it all over with her good old friend. The moment the child went into the house, Miss Dorner stepped out of the living room.

“You have just come in time,” she said, “for I have to show you something. Where are you going?”

“To the kitchen,” replied Cornelli.

“You have nothing whatever to do in the kitchen and you shall not go there. I thought you knew that you have to go upstairs before lunch to fix your hair. But before you go up come in here. I have to tell you something very important.”

Cornelli followed her cousin into the room. Miss Grideelen was standing near the window as if she had expected the return of her friend. Leading Cornelli to the sofa, Miss Dorner pointed to it, saying: “You are sure to know who has done this and you had better tell me right away.”

===Chinese===
回来的路上，科内莉一溜烟朝厩房跑去。可她猛地钉在了原地，因为她想起自己再也不许到那儿去了。那可爱的小山羊，她再也见不着了，再也瞧不着它一天天长大了。等到它长大套上小车、由她赶着到处跑的日子到了，她也无从知道。说不定连那样的事也不许她做呢！不过她盼着到那时父亲就回来了，一切也就会是另一副样子了。想到这儿，科内莉欢喜得直蹦，往日那股快活劲儿仿佛又回来了。她想到埃丝特那儿去，跟这位贴心的老朋友把这事儿痛痛快快聊一聊。孩子刚一进屋，多尔纳小姐正好从起居室里走了出来。

"你来得正好，"她说，"我有样东西要给你看看。你这是上哪儿去？"

"上厨房。"科内莉回答。

"厨房里的事跟你毫不相干，你也不许去。我还当你知道呢，午饭前得先上楼去把头发梳好。不过上楼之前，先到这儿来一下。我有件顶要紧的事得跟你说。"

科内莉跟着表姐走进房间。格里德伦小姐站在窗边，仿佛早就在等朋友回来似的。多尔纳小姐把科内莉领到沙发跟前，指着它说："这事是谁干的，你心里准有数，还是趁早告诉我的好。"

===Original===
On the dark plush coverings were visible distinct marks of dusty shoe soles. There was no trace of a whole foot, but one could see that somebody had trampled on the sofa.

“I did not do it,” said Cornelli with sparkling eyes.

“Who in all the house would have done it except you? Please ask yourself that, Cornelli! There is no question about it at all,” said Miss Dorner. “It is probably one of your little jokes similar to throwing your dresses out of the window. I know all about it. Just let me tell you this! It is the last time that you, a girl of ten years old, will show such a terrible lack of manners. As long as I am here, you shall not do it any more. You really should spare your good, sensitive father such behavior.”

“I have not done it. No, I did not do it, no, no!” Cornelli cried aloud.

“But Cornelli, only reflect! You are blushing and your conscience is giving you away,” Miss Grideelen here remarked. “It would be so much better for you to say humbly: ‘I have done it and I am sorry; I shall never do it again!’ ”

“No, no! I have not done it. No, no!” Cornelli cried out louder still. Her cheeks were glowing red from anger and excitement.

===Chinese===
深色的长毛绒沙发面上，清清楚楚印着几个沾灰的鞋底印子。虽然看不出一个完整的脚印，但看得出有人在沙发上踩踏过。

"不是我干的。"科内莉说着，眼睛里直冒火星。

"这满宅子上上下下，除了你还能有谁？你自己好好想想吧，科内莉！这还有什么可怀疑的。"多尔纳小姐说。"八成又是你的一个小把戏，跟把裙子扔出窗外是一路的。这些我全知道。你听我说！一个十岁的女孩子，这可是最后一回让你闹出这种毫无规矩的事来。只要我还在这儿，就不许你再犯。你也真该体谅体谅你那位善良敏感的父亲，别让他见到这种行为。"

"我没干。不，不是我干的，不是，不是！"科内莉大声喊起来。

"可是科内莉，你倒想想！你脸都红了，这是你的良心在出卖你。"格里德伦小姐这时开了口。"你要是能恭恭敬敬地说一句：'是我干的，我很抱歉，我再也不这样了！'那该多好。"

"不是，不是！我没干。不是，不是！"科内莉喊得更响了。她又气又急，两颊烧得通红。

===Original===
“Do not make such a noise,” ordered the cousin. “One might think there was an accident. It is not worth while to lose so many words. You should not have made things worse by denying it; if you had not, everything would be all settled. You have misbehaved and you shall not do so any more. Remember!”

“No, I did not misbehave. No, no! And I shall not say yes when it is not true,” Cornelli now cried, quite beside herself.

“Go to your room, Cornelli, and smooth out your forehead before you come to dinner. Your little horns are protruding quite plainly when you act that way. Just look at yourself in the mirror and see yourself how repulsive you look. If you think that there is anybody in the world who can still like you when you have black horns on your forehead, you are mistaken. Go, now, and return with another face.”

Cornelli went.

Reaching her room, Cornelli put her hand up to her brow. Right on her forehead were two protruding points. Should horns be really growing there? The child had a sudden horrible fright at this thought. She was sure that everybody could see them already, for she could feel them quite distinctly. She could not stand it any longer, so she ran away to old Martha.

===Chinese===
"不许这么嚷嚷。"表姐命令道。"人家还当出了什么大祸事。犯不上费这么多口舌。你不该抵赖，把事情越弄越糟；你要是不赖，这事早就了结了。你失了体面，往后不许再犯。记住了！"

"不，我没有失体面。没有，没有！不是真的事，我就不说'是'。"科内莉这会儿喊道，整个人简直完全失了自持。

"回你的房间去，科内莉，吃饭之前先把脑门抹平了。你一这么犯劲儿，你那两只小犄角就支棱得清清楚楚。自己照照镜子去，瞧瞧你这副模样有多讨厌。你要是以为天底下还有人能喜欢一个脑门上顶着黑犄角的孩子，那你就想错了。去吧，换另一张脸再来。"

科内莉走了。

回到自己的房间，科内莉抬手摸了摸额头。脑门上果真有两个凸起的尖儿。难道真是在长犄角了？这个念头让孩子猛地吓了一大跳。她断定人家一定全都看得见了，因为她自己分明摸得出来。她再也受不了啦，一口气跑去找老玛莎了。

===Original===
“No, I did not do it, Martha. I never did it,” she called out, running into the little room. “When I tell them no, no, they ought to believe that I did not do it. I never, never did it. They shall know it! But they won’t believe me even if I say it a hundred times and﻿—”

“Stop a little, Cornelli!” said old Martha kindly. “You see, you are all out of breath. Sit down here on your stool and tell me quietly what has excited you so. You know that I believe your words. I have known you since you were small, and I know that what you say is true.”

It was impossible for Cornelli to speak calmly about what had happened, but it soothed her, nevertheless, to be able to pour out her heart and to know that Martha believed her. She told of the accusation which had been brought against her, and how she had not been believed despite all her assurances. She was certain that both ladies would always believe forever and ever that she had done it and had denied it. At this thought Cornelli again became quite red from excitement and was on the point of breaking out again. But Martha put her hand on the child’s shoulder, quietly restraining her.

“No, no, Cornelli, that’s enough,” she said soothingly. “It is only to your advantage that it is so and not as they have said. You have been accused wrongly and cannot prove it, but God knows the truth. He has heard everything. You can be calm and happy and look up to Him with a clear conscience. You can say to yourself: ‘God knows it, and I do not need to be afraid or frightened.’ If you had really done wrong and had denied it, you would have to be afraid that the truth would be revealed. Then you could not look up calmly to the sky, for you would be frightened at the thought that up there was One who knew everything and from whom nothing could be hidden. A wrong accusation does not stay with us forever. Even if it takes ever so long, it generally is revealed in the end, and you certainly will not need to bear it in all eternity, because God already knows how it is.”

===Chinese===
"不是我干的，玛莎。我从来没那么干过。"她一头冲进小屋，嚷道。"我都跟她们说不是、不是了，她们就该相信我没干。我从来、从来没那么干过。她们必须知道这一点！可我说上一百遍她们也不会信，而且——"

"先缓一缓，科内莉！"老玛莎和蔼地说。"瞧你，气都喘不匀了。坐到你这张小凳子上，安安静静告诉我，是什么事把你急成这样。你知道我是信你的话的。你从丁点儿大我就认得你，我知道你说的都是真的。"

要科内莉心平气和地讲这件事是不可能的，不过能把心里的话一股脑儿倒出来，又知道玛莎信她，她总算得到了些安抚。她讲了人家怎样冤枉她，讲了尽管她百般分辩、人家怎么也不肯信她。她认定那两位小姐从此永远永远都会相信是她干的，还会相信她抵赖了。想到这里，科内莉又激动得满脸通红，眼看又要发作起来。玛莎却把手搭在孩子的肩上，静静地按住了她。

"好了，好了，科内莉，够了。"她抚慰道。"事情是这样、而不是她们说的那样，这对你只有好处。你受了冤枉，又没法证明自己，可是上帝知道真相。他什么都听见了。你可以安安心心、快快活活，问心无愧地仰望他。你可以对自己说：'上帝知道，我用不着害怕，用不着惊慌。'要是你真做错了事又抵赖了，那你就得提心吊胆，怕真相大白。那样的话，你就不能坦然地仰望天空了，因为一想到上头坐着一位无所不知、什么都瞒不过他的，你就会心惊胆战。冤枉不会跟着我们一辈子。哪怕拖得再久，到头来真相总会水落石出，你也决不至于生生世世背着这个冤屈，因为上帝早知道事情是怎样的。"

===Original===
Cornelli had really grown calm at the thought that there was One who knew how it all was. When her trouble began to weigh upon her, she could always say: “You know it all, dear Father in Heaven, You have seen and heard everything.”

“If He could only tell them! They would then know it, too. God could easily do that,” Cornelli said.

“Yes, but that is not the way things happen. We do not know better than He what is good for us,” Martha said, shaking her head quite seriously. “If we could rule, everything would come wrong. We never can see ahead of the hour and we never know what is good for us because the next moment always brings something we did not know about. Otherwise we would always be trying to undo what we have strained to do the day before; we should only make ourselves miserable over and over again. But if God ordains anything that we do not understand, we must believe firmly that something good will come out of it. We must be patient, and if our troubles are too heavy, we must console ourselves and think: God knows what good will come from it. But we are forgetting the time, Cornelli. You must hurry home to your dinner, now. I am afraid it is already late.”

Cornelli’s black frown had disappeared during Martha’s soothing speech, but now a deep shadow flew across her face.

===Chinese===
想到有一位知道全部实情的，科内莉真的平静下来了。往后苦恼再压上心头的时候，她总能说："亲爱的天父，这一切您都知道，您全都看见了、听见了。"

"他要是能告诉她们就好了！那她们也就知道了。这上帝轻而易举就办得到。"科内莉说。

"是啊，可事情不是那么办的。什么对我们好，我们不会比他知道得更清楚。"玛莎说着，极其郑重地摇了摇头。"要是让我们来当家，什么事都得砸。我们永远望不出这一个钟头去，也永远说不准什么对我们好，因为下一个时辰带来的，总是我们料不到的事。不然的话，我们就会成天忙着拆掉头一天拼死拼活做成的事，只会一遍又一遍地自寻苦恼。可要是上帝安排了我们不明白的事，我们就得坚信，那里面必有好结果。我们得忍耐，苦恼太重的时候，就宽宽自己的心，想想：上帝知道这事会结出什么好果子来。哟，咱们把时辰都忘了，科内莉。这会儿你得赶紧回家吃午饭去了。恐怕已经不早啦。"

玛莎这一番抚慰的话，把科内莉额头的乌云都说散了，可这时一片深深的暗影又掠过她的脸。

===Original===
“Oh, Martha, if I only did not need to go home any more! I hate to go back and sit at table. I would not mind dying of hunger, if I could only stay here with you.”

Cornelli, glancing at her home, drew together her brows as if she saw something frightful there.

“But, child, you must not say such things about your lovely home; it is wrong to do that,” said Martha, kindly admonishing her. “Just think how many children have no home at all. How grateful they would be to God for a home like yours. Go, now, Cornelli, be grateful for all God has given you and chase away the thoughts that make you sad. Come soon again and we shall be glad together, for there is always something to be glad about.”

Cornelli went. While she had been with Martha and had heard her words, it had really seemed to her that there was no cause for grief. As soon, however, as she entered the garden and saw the windows of the room where they were surely already at table, everything that had pressed heavily on her heart rose again. After all, Martha did not know everything.

===Chinese===
"哦，玛莎，要是再不用回家就好了！我不愿意回去坐在饭桌边上。哪怕饿死我也不怕，只要能跟你待在这儿。"

科内莉朝自己家望了一眼，眉头又拧了起来，仿佛在那边看见了什么可怕的东西。

"可是孩子，你不能这样说你自己那个可爱的家；这样说是不对的。"玛莎和颜悦色地劝她。"想想看，有多少孩子连个家都没有呢。要是能有一个你这样的家，他们该对上帝多么感恩哪。去吧，科内莉，为上帝给了你的这一切心怀感激，把那些叫你难过的心思统统赶跑。快些再来，咱们一块儿高兴高兴，因为总归有些事情是值得高兴的。"

科内莉走了。跟玛莎待在一块儿、听她说话的时候，她真的觉得没有什么可伤心的。可是她一走进园子，望见那间屋子的窗子——她们准已经围着桌子坐下了——沉甸甸压在她心头的一切又都涌了上来。玛莎到底不是什么都知道啊。

===Original===
Cornelli was sure that she could never be happy any more. She could not go in there and she could not eat. She felt as if she could not swallow anything, for big stones seemed to stick in her throat. If she would only die from it all! Cornelli thought that that would be best, for then everything would be over. So she sat down on the lawn behind the thick currant bushes, where she could not be seen from the house. Meanwhile, Miss Mina had carried away the sweets and was putting the fruit course on the table.

“It seems to me that Cornelli does not care if she comes to table a whole hour late,” said Miss Dorner. “Nothing is to be kept warm for her, for she does not seem to have learned yet how to respect time and order. She had better learn it soon.”

Mina went out to sit down for her dinner. Esther had everything ready and was just putting the dessert in the cupboard.

“That is for Cornelli as soon as she comes home,” she said, sitting down, too; “the poor child gets enough bitter things to swallow nowadays.”

===Chinese===
科内莉断定自己再也不会快活了。她进不去，也吃不下。她觉得什么都咽不下去，因为嗓子里像堵着一块块大石头。要是这一切能叫她死了才好呢！科内莉想，那样才最好，因为一切就都完了。于是她坐在草坪上、密密的红醋栗丛后面，宅子里谁也瞧不见的地方。这时候，米娜小姐已经撤下了甜食，正往桌上摆水果。

"依我看，科内莉是存心要晚整整一个钟头才上桌。"多尔纳小姐说。"什么也不要给她温着，她好像还没学会怎样尊重时间和规矩呢。这一课她还是早点学会的好。"

米娜出去坐下吃她自己的午饭。埃丝特把样样都拾掇好了，正把那份甜点放进碗橱。

"这是给科内莉留的，她一回家就端给她。"她也坐下来说，"这可怜的孩子，如今要咽下的苦东西已经够多的了。"

===Original===
“But why shouldn’t she come in time?” asked Mina crossly. “Besides, she couldn’t possibly eat the whole dessert. We can take our share and there will be enough left, surely as much as is good for her.”

“I won’t let you have it,” said Esther, firmly pressing her arm to the table as a sign that she would stay there. “The child must have something that will help her to swallow all the cross words she hears all day,” she continued. “What was wrong again this morning, when there was such a scene in the living room?”

“It was nothing,” replied Mina. “There were a few marks of dust on the sofa, and the ladies thought that Cornelli had been standing on it. The child would not admit it and so the ladies kept on accusing her till Cornelli set up a senseless row.”

“I really think, Miss Mina, that you could have given an explanation,” said Esther with a sly smile. “If one has to wind up the clock, it is quicker to jump up on the sofa than to push the heavy thing away. When one wears tight lace boots in the early morning, one can’t take them off easily, eh, Miss Mina?” With these words Esther glanced at the neat little boots that Mina was stretching out comfortably under the table.

===Chinese===
"可她干吗不按时来呢？"米娜气呼呼地问。"再说，那么一大份甜点，她一个人也吃不完。咱们拿走自己的一份，剩下的照样够，肯定够她吃的了。"

"我不许你们动它。"埃丝特说着，把胳膊往桌上一按，表示她要守在那儿。"这孩子总得有点什么，帮着她咽下她一整天听见的那些恶言恶语。"她接着说，"今天早晨又是怎么了，起居室里闹了那么一场？"

"没什么。"米娜回答。"沙发上落了几个带灰的鞋印子，两位小姐就认定是科内莉踩上去的。孩子不肯认，她们就没完没了地数落她，数落到后来，科内莉就撒起泼来了。"

"我可觉得，米娜小姐，你本可以出来说明说明的。"埃丝特脸上带着一丝狡黠的笑说。"人要给钟上弦，跳上沙发总比挪开那件沉家伙快当。一大早穿着紧巴巴的系带小靴子，脱起来多费事，是不是，米娜小姐？"说这话时，埃丝特瞟了一眼米娜舒舒服服伸在桌子底下的那双精致小靴。

===Original===
“Well, what was there so terrible about that?” retorted Mina pertly. “The sofa won’t be spoiled on account of that, and besides, I have to clean it myself.”

“I only think you could have said a word, before the ladies accused the child of having lied to them and before she nearly had a fit over the injustice. She made such a noise that one could hear it all over the house! It went right through me.”

“Oh, pooh! it was not as bad as that,” asserted Mina; “the child has long since forgotten the whole thing. That is the way with children. One moment they make a horrible noise and the next they go out of the door and forget about it. Why should one bother?”

“It used to be different,” said Esther smilingly, “Miss Mina could not be obliging enough to the child then. Things are all done for other people now and not for those of the house.”

===Chinese===
"嗐，那有什么了不得的？"米娜轻飘飘地顶了回去。"沙发又不会为这个就糟蹋了，再说，弄脏了还不是我自己擦。"

"我只是觉得，你本该言语一声的——趁两位小姐还没数落孩子撒了谎、趁她还没让这份冤枉气得几乎背过气去的时候。她闹得那么凶，满宅子都听得见！听得我心里直发颤。"

"嗐，得了吧！没那么严重。"米娜一口咬定，"孩子早把整件事忘到脑后去了。孩子就是这个样子。这一会儿闹得天翻地覆，下一会儿出了门就忘个精光。何必往心里去呢？"

"从前可不一样，"埃丝特笑眯眯地说，"那时候，米娜小姐对孩子要多殷勤有多殷勤。如今啊，什么都为外人操办，自己家里的人倒不算数了。"

===Original===
“Those of the house!” repeated Mina mockingly. “It won’t be long before you, too, will be singing another tune. When the new lady of the house gives orders in the kitchen you will have to obey, too.”

Esther dropped her spoon. “For goodness sake, what are you saying?” she exclaimed. “Who should have thought of such a thing? Whom do you mean, the cousin or the other one?”

“Well, I can’t tell that exactly,” replied the maid. “Our master has not discussed that with me, but one must be dumb not to see what is going on and why the ladies came here. After all, one wants to know what one is going to do. That two have come, is the surest sign of all, for we shall be supposed not to suspect.”

“For goodness sake,” said Esther again, “what a discovery! I am sure it must be the relation, for she already rules the house. I tell you one thing, though, Miss Mina, that I shall keep on singing the same tune I have been singing for the last twelve years in this house, and I don’t care who is going to rule. You can believe me.”

===Chinese===
"自己家里的人！"米娜讥讽地学舌。"用不了多久，你也要换一副腔调喽。等这家的新女主人来厨房里发号施令，你也得听她的。"

埃丝特手里的勺子当啷一声掉了下来。"老天爷，你在说些什么呀？"她喊道。"谁会想到这样的事？你指的是谁，是表姐，还是另外那一位？"

"嗐，这个我也说不准。"女仆回答。"咱们的主人可没跟我商量过这事。可是眼下在闹腾什么、两位小姐为什么到这儿来，除非是个哑巴才看不出来。说到底，谁不想知道自己往后要伺候谁呢。一来就来了两位，这就是最确实不过的凭据，因为人家就是打算让咱们不起疑心。"

"老天爷，"埃丝特又说了一遍，"这可真是件大发现！我敢保准是那位亲戚，因为这宅子如今已经是她在当家了。不过有一句话我跟你说定了，米娜小姐：这十二年我在这个家里唱的是什么调子，往后我还照旧唱下去，管他后来谁来当家。你只管信我。"

===Original===
“Oh, we shall see about that, Esther,” said Mina with a superior air. She got up, now, to see if the ladies needed anything.

Waking up from a sound sleep, Cornelli did not remember where she was. She was lying on the lawn behind the currant bushes. She remembered at last how she had come back at lunch time from Martha’s cottage and how she had suddenly felt weary and sleepy. She must have dropped down and gone to sleep.

It was evening and there was no more sunshine on the grass, but the sky was still light, although it was beginning to grow dark. Cornelli suddenly had a longing she had never known before. She felt as if she had to eat and taste everything about her, the bushes and the leaves, the flowers, and especially the unripe plums on the tree above her. Oh, if she only had a piece of bread! Cornelli got up quickly and ran towards the house.

“Come quickly, Cornelli,” Esther called to her through the open kitchen window; “they are just sitting down to supper; you have come just in time.”

===Chinese===
"哼，咱们走着瞧吧，埃丝特。"米娜一副高人一等的神气说。她这时起身去看看两位小姐有什么要吩咐的。

科内莉从酣睡中醒来，一时想不起自己身在哪儿。她正躺在红醋栗丛后面的草地上。她终于想起来，午饭时分她怎样从玛莎的小屋回来，又怎样突然觉得又累又困。她准是就地一倒就睡着了。

已经是傍晚了，草地上早没了阳光，可天空还亮着，虽然天色已经渐渐暗下来。科内莉忽然涌起一股从来没有过的馋劲儿。她觉得自己非得把身边的东西都吃一吃、尝一尝不可——那些树丛和叶子，那些花，尤其是头顶那棵树上还没熟的李子。哦，要是有一块面包该多好！科内莉一骨碌爬起来，朝屋子跑去。

"快来，科内莉，"埃丝特从敞开的厨房窗子里朝她喊，"她们正要坐下吃晚饭呢；你来得正是时候。"

===Original===
Cornelli flew to her room and, pulling out a thick shawl from among her things, tied it around her head. Then, running to the dining room, she sat down at her accustomed seat.

“So you have come again,” said Miss Dorner, who had just settled down, too. “A well brought up child should at least say good evening when she enters the room after a long absence.”

“Good evening,” said Cornelli, after which she finished her soup with unusual haste.

“Where do you come from after all this time?” asked the cousin.

“From the garden,” was the reply.

===Chinese===
科内莉飞奔到自己的房间，从她的东西堆里抽出一条厚厚的披巾，把头裹了起来。然后跑进饭厅，在她惯常的座位上坐下。

"哦，你到底又露面了。"刚刚落座的多尔纳小姐说。"一个有教养的孩子，出去了老半天，一进屋总该道一声晚上好。"

"晚上好。"科内莉应了一声，随后便格外飞快地把她的汤喝完了。

"去了这么半天，你到底从哪儿来？"表姐问。

"从园子里来。"回答就这么一句。

===Original===
“That is quite possible, but where were you before that?”

“With Martha,” Cornelli answered.

“If you could only learn to answer more pleasantly!” remarked Miss Dorner, “it would be to your own advantage, for you do not have many pleasing things about you; it would only make you more attractive, and you really should strive to become so.

“Next time you want to stay so long at this woman’s house you have to ask my permission. I absolutely forbid you to stay away so long without asking me, do you hear? You deserve to be scolded for your long absence today, but I shall not say anything further. But why do you look so pitiful! What is the matter? Have you a toothache?”

===Chinese===
"这倒不假，可在那之前你在哪儿？"

"在玛莎那儿。"科内莉回答。

"你要是能学着回话回得中听些就好了！"多尔纳小姐说，"这对你自己也有好处，因为你身上招人喜欢的地方本就不多；那样只会让你更招人待见，你也实在该朝这个方向努力才是。

"下回你再到这个女人家里待这么久，得先问过我。不经我许可就走开这么长时间，我绝对不许，听见没有？今天耽搁这么久，本该骂你一顿，可我不再多说了。可你怎么哭丧着一张脸！怎么了？牙疼吗？"

===Original===
“No,” Cornelli quickly gave forth.

“Have you a headache?”

“No.”

“What is the matter with you?”

“Nothing.”

===Chinese===
"不疼。"科内莉飞快地吐出一个字。

"头疼吗？"

"不疼。"

"那你到底怎么了？"

"没怎么。"

===Original===
“You shall never again set up such a masquerade when there is nothing the matter with you, Cornelli,” said the cousin scoldingly. “Why do you put this shawl around your head? Are you trying to look like an untidy gypsy? Don’t ever come to table that way again! Betty, have you ever seen the like? Can you understand this behavior from a sensible child?”

The friend just shook her head.

“Perhaps Cornelli does it because she does not know what else to do. She does not seem to desire a proper occupation,” she replied.

When Cornelli came down to breakfast next day, she had taken off the shawl, but she still looked very odd.

===Chinese===
"明明没什么事，却摆出这么一套化装把戏，往后可再不许了，科内莉。"表姐训斥道。"你干吗把披巾裹在头上？你是想扮个邋里邋遢的吉卜赛女人吗？再也别这副模样上桌了！贝蒂，你见过这样的吗？一个明白事理的孩子，你能理解这种行径吗？"

朋友只是摇了摇头。

"也许科内莉这么做，是因为她不知道还能干什么。她好像并不想要一份正经的营生。"她回答。

第二天科内莉下来吃早饭时，披巾倒是摘了，模样却还是怪得很。

===Original===
“You look exactly like a savage from New Zealand,” said the cousin. “Do you think you are improving your appearance by plastering your hair all over your face?”

“No,” said Cornelli fiercely.

“Neither do I,” said the cousin. “I cannot make you out at all. What will you put on next, I wonder, when your hair is brushed away?”

“My fur cap,” replied Cornelli, according to the truth.

“I never heard such nonsense,” exclaimed Miss Dorner. “I really think that the child is capable of doing that. She will probably pull it down over her head to her nose when the temperature is eighty. I have never seen such a child. What shall I do with her?”

===Chinese===
"你这模样，活脱儿是个新西兰来的野人。"表姐说。"你以为把头发糊得满脸都是，就能好看到哪儿去吗？"

"不觉得。"科内莉恶狠狠地说。

"我也不觉得。"表姐说。"我简直摸不透你。我倒要看看，等你的头发给你刷开以后，你下一回又要在头上扣个什么？"

"我的皮帽子。"科内莉照实回答。

"我从没听见过这种混账话。"多尔纳小姐喊道。"我真觉得这孩子干得出来。没准儿天气热到八十度的时候，她真会把皮帽子拉下来，一直扣到鼻子上。这样的孩子我从没见过。我拿她可怎么办？"

===Original===
Cornelli really looked as if she did not know how well brought up European girls usually wore their hair. From the middle of her head thick uneven strands of dark hair hung down over her forehead and deep into her eyes. The hair was not hanging loose, but was firmly glued to her skin. Her intention seemed to be to keep it there to prevent it from being blown away.

“You look positively repulsive and no person on earth will want to look at you if you go around like that. This may teach you to give up your terrible obstinacy! Nothing else can be done with you.”

With these words the cousin rose and left the room. Miss Grideelen promptly followed.

That evening a letter was sent to Cornelli’s father:

===Chinese===
科内莉这副模样，真像是压根儿不知道有教养的欧洲姑娘通常是怎么梳头发的。一绺绺又厚又不齐整的黑发，从她头顶正中披下来，盖住她的额头，直垂进眼睛里。头发不是松松地悬着，而是牢牢地粘在皮肤上。看那意思，她是要把它们稳稳当当留在那儿，好不被风吹跑。

"你这模样实实在在叫人恶心，你这副打扮走动，天底下没有一个人愿意多看你一眼。但愿这能教你改掉那可怕的犟脾气！对你真是再没有别的法子可想了。"

说完这些话，表姐站起身出了房间。格里德伦小姐也紧跟着走了出去。

当天晚上，一封信寄给了科内莉的父亲：

===Original===
Iller-Stream,

					July 20th, 18﻿—.

My dear Cousin:

Your affairs are going brilliantly, for your manager is splendid. I can also inform you that perfect order reigns in your house, your garden and the stable. Your place is perfectly magnificent; it abounds in fruit and vegetables and lovely flowers. I should never have imagined this possible years ago, when I wandered about here with my friend Cornelia.

===Chinese===
伊勒溪，

					7月20日，18——年。

亲爱的表兄：

您的事业蒸蒸日上，因为您的经理人十分出色。我还可以奉告：您的宅子、园子和厩房，处处井然有序。您这处家业实在壮丽极了，水果蔬菜、姹紫嫣红的花，样样丰饶。几年前我同我的朋友科内莉亚在这里漫步的时候，怎么也想象不到会有今日。

===Original===
I am coming now to the principal subject of this letter, which is less pleasant. I do not understand how your daughter has gotten her disposition. She does not either resemble you, with your fresh and open manner, or Cornelia, with her merry, pliant disposition, which won everyone’s heart. The child has a dull and sullen nature, a roughness of manner and an unheard-of stubbornness. I can do nothing for her, at least not by anything I say. But I have decided to leave physical or other punishment to you. I shall do all I can by good example and admonishment as long as I am here. My friend is supporting me faithfully. I do not dare raise in you the hope that the child will ever make you happy. A rebellious nature like hers is sure to get worse from year to year. I hope, however, that the success of all your ventures will give you the satisfaction that your home life cannot give you.

Your faithful cousin,

					**Kitty Dorner**.

===Chinese===
现在我要来谈这封信的正题了，这就不那么愉快了。我不明白，令嫒的性子是怎么来的。她既不像您——您那样爽朗坦率；也不像科内莉亚——她那样快活柔顺，人人都为她倾心。这孩子天性沉闷乖戾，举止粗鲁，倔强得闻所未闻。我拿她毫无办法，至少光凭我嘴上说的是不管用的。不过，体罚也好，别的责罚也好，我决意留给您来施行。只要我还在这儿，我定当以身作则、谆谆劝诫，尽力而为。我的朋友在忠心耿耿地帮衬我。我不敢让您生出指望，以为这孩子日后还能给您带来欢乐。像她这样反叛的天性，只会一年坏似一年。不过我仍希望，您各项事业的兴旺发达，能给您带来家庭生活所不能给的满足。

您忠实的表妹，

					**基蒂·多尔纳（Kitty Dorner）**。

## V / 五

A Newcomer in Iller-Stream / 伊勒溪来的新客人

===Original===
Old Matthew was raking the gravel paths in the garden when Cornelli stepped out of the house and slowly approached. She held a book in her hand and now sat down on the bench under the hazel bush. Laying the book on her lap, she watched Matthew while he cleaned up the paths. Looking up he said: “Come with me, Cornelli, and let us go over to the stable together, for you have not been there for a long time. You should see how the little kid is growing.”

Cornelli merely shook her head and gave no answer. Matthew looked over at the child a few more times, but said no more.

Esther, carrying a large basket, now arrived. As she was going to the vegetable garden she called over to the child: “You must have a specially nice book to be sitting there so quietly, Cornelli.”

Cornelli shook her head.

“No?” laughed Esther. “All right, then, come with me and I’ll show you how many yellow plums there are going to be this year; the whole tree is full and they are already beginning to ripen.”

“I don’t care,” said Cornelli.

===Chinese===
老马修正在园子里耙着砾石小径，科内莉从屋里出来，慢慢地走近。她手里拿着一本书，在榛树丛下的长凳上坐了下来。她把书摊在膝上，望着马修收拾小径。马修抬起头来，说：“跟我来吧，科内莉，我们一起到马厩那边去，你可是好久没去那儿了。你该去看看那只小羊羔长得多大了。”

科内莉只是摇了摇头，没有答话。马修又朝孩子望了好几眼，也就不再说什么了。

这时埃丝特挎着一只大篮子来了。她正要往菜园去，隔着老远冲孩子喊道：“科内莉，你能这么安安静静地坐在那儿，手里拿的准是一本特别好的书吧。”

科内莉摇了摇头。

“不是吗？”埃丝特笑道。“那好，跟我来，我带你去看看今年能结多少黄李子；满树都是，眼看就要熟了。”

“我才不稀罕呢。”科内莉说。

===Original===
“No?” laughed Esther. “All right, then, plums,” Esther exclaimed. “And our large juicy pears are beginning to get ripe, too. Don’t you want to come and see how long it will be before they are ripe?”

“No,” was the reply.

Esther now went her ways. A short time after that Matthew joined her. “What is the matter with the child, Esther,” he asked. “She is so changed! One can hardly recognize any more our gay and friendly Cornelli. And why does she have her hair hanging into her face that way? One absolutely does not know her any more.”

“That is just what I say,” Esther replied. “I really can’t understand it. One hardly ever sees the child, and if one does meet her somewhere, she scarcely says a word. She never sings or laughs the way she used to, and she always wears such a terribly unhappy expression that it fairly makes one’s heart ache. How happy the child used to be!

“They say that she needs to be educated, and it may be so; but since she is getting an education she is absolutely changed, and not for the better. However, things may go well again when her education is finished.”

“She misses her mother,” said Matthew. “It is awfully hard on a little one to grow up without a mother, for she needs her at every step. It is so easy when you have a mother to whom you can tell your joys and troubles.”

===Chinese===
“不是吗？”埃丝特又笑了。“那好，李子就算了。”埃丝特嚷道。“咱们那水灵灵的大梨也开始熟了。你不想来看看，它们还得多久才能熟透吗？”

“不想。”回答就这么两个字。

埃丝特自去了。过了不大一会儿，马修凑到她跟前。“这孩子到底怎么了，埃丝特？”他问。“她变得太厉害了！咱们那个快快活活、见人就亲的科内莉，都快认不出来了。她干吗让头发那么垂在脸上？简直叫人再也认不出她来。”

“我也正这么说呢。”埃丝特答道。“这事我真弄不明白。这孩子几乎见不着影儿，偶尔在哪儿碰上了，她也难得说上一句。她再不像从前那样又唱又笑，脸上总挂着那么一副愁苦得吓人的神情，看得人心口发疼。这孩子从前多快活啊！

“人家说她得受受教育，兴许是这么回事；可自打她受了教育，她就整个儿变了样，还越变越糟。不过，等她这教育受完了，兴许又会好起来的。”

“她是想妈妈了。”马修说。“小孩子没有母亲，长大可太不容易了，因为她步步都离不开她呀。有个母亲，什么欢喜什么烦恼都能说给她听，那是多省心的事。”

===Original===
“One might think that you still run to your mother whenever anyone does you harm, Matthew,” said Esther, a little mockingly.

“I should love to,” Matthew assured her. “I know what my mother meant to me and so I am always sorry for every child that has none. One can see how it is with our master’s child; nothing is of any good to her as long as she has no mother.”

Matthew went away, looking once more with pity at Cornelli, who was sitting quite motionless on the bench. The book by now was lying on the ground.

Soon afterwards Mr. Maelinger entered the garden and neared the house, but Cornelli intercepted him.

“I could not come at 9 o’clock today,” he said, “but I think one hour is better than none, so am here now, at 11 o’clock. I hope you have spent a pleasant, useful morning.”

===Chinese===
“听你这话，人家还当你如今受了谁的欺负，还要跑去找你妈妈诉苦呢，马修。”埃丝特说，带着几分打趣。

“要是能那样，我巴不得呢。”马修一口咬定。“我知道我妈妈对我意味着什么，所以每逢哪个孩子没了娘，我总是心疼。咱们东家孩子的事，就是明摆着的例子；她一天没有母亲，什么好处对她都是白搭。”

马修走开了，又怜惜地回头望了科内莉一眼——她还一动不动地坐在长凳上，那本书这会儿已经掉在了地上。

过了不久，梅林格先生（Mr. Maelinger）走进园子，朝房子这边过来，却在半道上被科内莉拦住了。

“今天九点钟我来不成，”他说，“不过我想，上一个钟头的课，总比一个钟头都不上强，所以现在十一点，我来了。但愿你这一上午过得又愉快又有益。”

===Original===
“No, I haven’t,” said Cornelli drily.

“But you have a fine book in your hand. It is sure to have something nice in it. What is it all about?”

“I do not know,” replied Cornelli.

“Let us go to our work now. Your reading does not seem to have impressed you much, so let us hope for a better result from our lesson.”

The teacher entered the house with his pupil, and they were just getting settled in their accustomed places when he said: “It seems to me, Cornelli, that your hair hangs a little too much over your face. It must be very uncomfortable. Could not this be changed?”

“No, I can never change that, never, never,” Cornelli said passionately, tightly pressing down the hair on her forehead. “Oh, really! But this is no affair of mine,” said the teacher calmly. “Only it seems to me a rather disfiguring manner of wearing the hair. You would feel much more comfortable without these weeping-willow-like hangings in front of your eyes.”

===Chinese===
“不，没有。”科内莉干巴巴地说。

“可你手里不是拿着一本好书吗？里面准有些好东西。它讲的是什么呀？”

“我不知道。”科内莉回答。

“那我们现在开始做功课吧。你读的书好像没让你起多大的劲头，但愿咱们这堂课能收个好一点的效果。”

老师带着他的学生走进屋子，两人刚在各自惯常的位子上坐定，他就开口了：“依我看，科内莉，你的头发垂到脸上来的，未免多了一点儿。那一定很不舒服吧。这个就不能改一改吗？”

“不，这个我永远改不了，永远，永远改不了。”科内莉激动地说，把额前的头发紧紧按住。“哦，真是的！这不关我的事。”老师平静地说。“只是依我看，这样梳头发未免有点儿毁人模样。眼前要是没有这些垂柳似的头发帘子，你会舒服得多。”

===Original===
Cornelli was still pressing both her hands against her forehead, as if the teacher might try by force to straighten up her hair. But he now began the lesson quite peacefully.

When the ladies were leaving the room after lunch, the cousin said to the child: “You are not going to run off again immediately, Cornelli. You must begin a proper and orderly existence. When your work is done you can read one of your many lovely books. You have enough time after our coffee hour to take walks and to pay visits.”

As usual the work was soon finished. Afterwards Cornelli sat down on the garden bench. Just as before, she put the book in her lap, and it soon fell to the ground. Cornelli peeped about her, at the trees and at the ground, but she did not really seem to see them.

At coffee time Cornelli punctually appeared at table and quickly gulped down everything that was poured out for her, as if it were a medicine that simply had to be swallowed. Afterwards she sat there frowning, for she had to remain at her seat till the ladies got up; she had learned this custom from her cousin.

“Don’t always frown and make such horns! One can see them quite plainly even through your curtains,” said Miss Dorner. “It won’t be long before you can go away.”

===Chinese===
科内莉仍旧双手捂着前额，仿佛老师要动硬的来给她把头发理直似的。可他这会儿已经心平气和地上起课来。

午饭过后，两位女士起身离席，表姐对孩子说：“这回你可不许马上又溜跑了，科内莉。你得开始过一种规规矩矩、有条有理的日子。功课做完了，可以从你那一大堆好看的书里挑一本读读。喝过咖啡之后，你有的是工夫散散步、拜访拜访人家。”

功课照例很快就做完了。随后科内莉坐到园子里的长凳上。跟先前一样，她把书往膝上一搁，不多会儿书就掉到了地上。科内莉东张张、西望望，看看树，又看看地上，其实什么都没有看进去。

到了喝咖啡的时辰，科内莉准时上了桌，把给她倒的各样东西一股脑儿往嘴里咽，就像喝一味非喝不可的药。喝完，她便皱着眉坐在那里，因为不等到女士们起身，她就得一直守在座位上；这个规矩，她是跟表姐学的。

“别老是皱着眉头、竖起两道犄角来！隔着你的头发帘子，人家都看得清清楚楚。”多尔纳小姐说。“也用不了多大工夫，你就可以走开了。”

===Original===
At last the ladies got up to go into the garden. Cornelli sneaked out behind them, turned unseen around the corner of the house, and walked across the meadow to the path.

“To sit here under the hazel bush and read a fine book is really a pleasure not many children have,” said Miss Dorner, sitting down on the bench. “For this alone you should be grateful, instead of frowning and sulking all day, Cornelli﻿—yes! But where has she gone again?” the lady interrupted herself, glancing around.

“She disappeared as soon as we came out,” her friend answered. “Isn’t Cornelli really peculiar? She never says a friendly word and never gives a single sign of childish love. She always runs away as soon as she possibly can.”

“I am so sorry for her father, who must long for a pleasant family life,” Miss Dorner continued. “He will never have this by the side of his only daughter, who seems to become more unfriendly and stubborn every day. Others in the house have noticed it, too, so Mina tells me. Oh, what a life it will be here in two or three years. My poor cousin with his beautiful estate! What good is that to him?”

“Many things can happen in two years that can’t be foretold, Kitty, and that can change a household entirely,” replied the other lady. “For the benefit of your cousin let us hope that this may come true.”

===Chinese===
女士们终于起身往园子里去了。科内莉跟在后面溜了出来，趁人不注意绕过屋角，穿过草地上了一条小路。

“能坐在这榛树丛底下读一本好书，实在是一般孩子享受不到的乐事。”多尔纳小姐在长凳上坐下，说道。“单为这一样，你就该知足感激了，不该成天皱着眉头赌气，科内莉——咦！她又跑到哪儿去了？”这位女士话说到一半忽然打住，四下张望起来。

“我们一出来，她就没影儿了。”她的朋友答道。“科内莉这孩子是不是真有点儿怪？她从不说一句亲热话，也从不露一点儿孩子气的爱意。只要能溜，她总是拔腿就溜。”

“我真替她父亲难过，他该多么盼着一份和和美美的家庭生活啊。”多尔纳小姐接着说。“守着这么个独生女儿，他是永远盼不上的；这孩子看上去一天比一天不亲热，一天比一天倔。家里旁人也都有所觉察，米娜就是这么告诉我的。唉，再过两三年，这家里的日子可怎么过哟。我那可怜的表兄，守着这么漂亮的庄园！这对他又有什么用呢？”

“两年里头，什么样的事都可能发生，基蒂，谁也料不定，说不定就把一户人家整个儿变了样。”另一位女士回答。“为了你表兄好，我们就盼着这话应验吧。”

===Original===
Cornelli was not leaping or running, but was quietly creeping along the edge of the path. She was staring at the ground, without once looking up at the merry birds which were whistling above her. Not once did she glance to right or left in the meadows, though they were full of red daisies and blue forget-me-nots which Cornelli ordinarily loved to pick.

Martha saw the approaching child. She came out with a worried face and full of sympathy asked: “What is wrong with you, Cornelli? Can you never again be merry?”

“No, not any more,” replied Cornelli, entering Martha’s little chamber and sitting down on the stool which her old friend had put for her in the usual place. Cornelli’s words did not come rapidly and angrily any more, as they had done before. With a deep sigh she added: “I only wish I had never learned to read.”

“What! But child, what an idea,” exclaimed Martha, “what a foolish wish! You should realize what it means to want to find out something and not be able to. One has to begin over and over again, and nothing helps one. That is what happened to me today. If you don’t help me I won’t ever understand it. I often wish I could read and write as fast as our Cornelli does. It is a great gift to be able to read and write easily, and everybody who can’t do it knows that well. Don’t you like the pretty books your father has given you?”

“No, I don’t. They are pretty, but awfully tiresome, Martha,” Cornelli assured her. “There are all kinds of stories and descriptions in them of famous people and discoveries. Father said that he used to love them when he was young, but he was probably different from me. Now I can’t run to the stable any more, nor into the woods as I feel like doing; now I have to sit around all the time and read a book. Oh, I wish nobody had written any books, then nobody would have to read them.”

===Chinese===
科内莉没有蹦，也没有跑，只顺着小路边悄悄地往前蹭。她眼睛盯着地面，头顶上欢快的鸟儿吹着口哨啼啭，她一次也不抬头看。两侧的草地上开满了红雏菊和蓝色的勿忘我——往常科内莉顶爱采它们了——她也一眼不往两边瞧。

玛莎望见了走近来的孩子。她一脸忧色地迎出来，满怀怜惜地问：“你怎么了，科内莉？你就再也不能快活起来了吗？”

“是的，再也不能了。”科内莉回答，走进玛莎的小屋，在老朋友照例给她放好的那只小凳上坐下。她的话不像从前那样又急又冲了。她深深地叹了口气，又添上一句：“我只盼着自己从来没学过认字。”

“什么！孩子，瞧你这念头。”玛莎嚷道，“多傻的愿望啊！你该明白，想弄明白一件事却弄不明白，那是什么滋味。得翻来覆去一遍又一遍地来，可怎么也帮不上自己。我今天就是这样。你要是不帮我，我永远也弄不明白了。我常常巴望着，自己能像咱们科内莉这样，读书写字又快又好。能轻轻松松地读书写字，是一样大本事，凡是不会的人，心里最清楚。你父亲给你的那些好看的书，你就不喜欢吗？”

“不，不喜欢。书是好看，可闷死人了，玛莎。”科内莉一口咬定。“里面全是种种故事和描写，讲的是名人啊、新发现啊。父亲说他年轻的时候可爱看它们了，可他大概跟我不一样。现在我再不能跑到马厩去，也不能想上林子就上林子了；现在我只得成天坐着，捧着一本书读。唉，我巴不得谁都没写过书，那样谁也就用不着读它们了。”

===Original===
“But Cornelli, I do not think that this would suit everybody,” Martha said. “Please help me to read a letter I got today, and then you will see what an advantage it is to be able to read. I need your help, for I do not understand what is wanted of me.”

Cornelli, taking up the letter, was quite willing to help her dear old friend.

“Who wrote it?” asked the child.

“That is just the thing I cannot read,” Martha answered. “I only know that it comes from town, but I cannot guess who could possibly write to me from there.”

Cornelli began to read the letter aloud. It was an inquiry as to whether the spare room had yet been taken, and if Mrs. Wolf could take care of a boy of twelve years for a few weeks. He did not need special care, as he was not exactly ill; but the boy undoubtedly was not very strong. Good air and fresh milk were the chief things he needed. If no refusal came, the boy would arrive in the middle of July. It was signed: Nika Halm, rector’s widow.

===Chinese===
“不过科内莉，我看这愿望搁在人人身上，未必都合适。”玛莎说。“请你帮我念念今天收到的一封信，到时候你就明白，能识字是多占便宜的事。我需要你帮忙，因为我弄不明白人家要我做什么。”

科内莉拿起那封信，很乐意帮亲爱的老朋友这个忙。

“谁写的呀？”孩子问。

“偏偏这一点，我就认不出来。”玛莎回答。“我只知道它是城里寄来的，可我猜不出那儿会有谁给我写信。”

科内莉开始大声念信。信上问的是那间空屋租出去了没有，沃尔夫太太能不能照看一个十二岁的男孩几个星期。他不算真的病着，用不着特别的照护；可这孩子身子骨无疑不太结实。他顶要紧的是好空气和新鲜牛奶。如果不曾回绝，孩子七月中旬就到。信的落款是：妮卡·哈尔姆，牧师遗孀。

===Original===
“Oh, how easily you read. It seems to go all of itself,” said Martha admiringly, when Cornelli had finished. “I never could have made it out so well. Just think how proud I can be that a rector’s wife will bring her son to me. Oh, I’ll take the best care of him, and I must ask Matthew to let him have some milk from the cows every morning and evening. Isn’t it too bad it is not a girl; then you would have a playmate. But you will entertain each other just the same. Are you not a little bit glad that he is coming?”

“No, not a bit,” Cornelli returned curtly. “I know quite well that he won’t have anything to do with me, and I know why, too. I do not care whether it is a boy or a girl. I don’t want him.”

“But Cornelli, you never used to be that way. You used to be so friendly and bright with everybody. What has happened to you?” asked Martha, quite grieved. “You do not look about you with bright eyes and your hair hangs too low on your face. Can’t I push it back a little?”

Martha, fetching a comb, was going to touch Cornelli’s hair, when Cornelli hindered her by crying out: “No, Martha, leave it! It has to stay that way all my life.”

“Oh, no, I won’t believe that. Why should your face be half covered up? One can hardly recognize you,” Martha said regretfully. “What do the ladies say about it?”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，你念得多顺当，那些字就好像自己蹦出来似的。”科内莉念完了，玛莎赞叹道。“我就怎么也念不了这么明白。你想想，一位牧师太太要把儿子送到我这儿来，我能不觉得体面吗！哦，我一定好好照看他，还得求马修让他每天早晚都从奶牛那儿得些奶来。可惜来的不是个女孩子，不然你就有个玩伴了。不过你们照样能一块儿玩得痛快的。他要来了，你就一点儿也不高兴吗？”

“不，一点儿也不。”科内莉没好气地回了一句。“我知道得很，他才不会理我呢，我也知道为什么。男孩女孩我都无所谓。我不要他来。”

“可是科内莉，你从前从来不这样。你从前见了谁都是又亲热又伶俐。你到底怎么了？”玛莎问，心里很难过。“你也不用亮晶晶的眼睛四下里看了，头发又垂得那么低，罩住了脸。让我给你往后拢一拢，不行吗？”

玛莎取来一把梳子，正要去碰科内莉的头发，科内莉拦住她，喊了起来：“不，玛莎，别动它！它就得一辈子这样。”

“哦，不，我才不信呢。好好一张脸，干吗要遮去一半？都快叫人认不出你了。”玛莎惋惜地说。“两位女士对这事怎么说？”

===Original===
“Miss Dorner says that I am the most obstinate being in the whole world, and that no one can ever set me right,” was Cornelli’s truthful information. Then she added: “She says that no child on earth looks as ugly as I do and that nobody in the world will ever like me. I know that it is true, and I only wish nobody were coming to you; then I could always be alone with you.”

“Cornelli, I am quite sure that you would do right in obeying the ladies,” said Martha. “If you did what they say, they would love you as well as everybody else does.”

“No, no, Martha, you don’t know how it is,” Cornelli said, quite frightened. “I’ll do everything they say, but I can never push my hair away, for then it would be worse still and everybody could see it.”

Martha shook her head.

“I do not know what you mean, Cornelli. Please come to me just as often as you can. I shall always love you more than anybody who might ever come here. If you did not come, it would hurt me dreadfully. Then I would rather not have the rector’s son here, glad as I am now that he is coming.”

===Chinese===
“多尔纳小姐说，我是全世界最执拗的人，谁也把我纠正不过来。”科内莉如实相告。接着她又补上一句：“她说，天底下没有哪个孩子长得像我这么丑，世界上也永远不会有人喜欢我。我知道这是真的。我只盼着谁也别上你这儿来；那样我就能一直单独跟你在一块儿了。”

“科内莉，我敢肯定，听两位女士的话，总是对的。”玛莎说。“你要是照她们说的去做，她们也会像旁人一样疼你的。”

“不，不，玛莎，你不知道是怎么回事。”科内莉说，吓得不轻。“她们说什么我都照办，可头发我永远不能撩开，因为那样只会更糟，人人就都看得见了。”

玛莎摇了摇头。

“我不明白你的意思，科内莉。求你尽量常来我这儿。不管往后有谁到这儿来，我永远最疼你。你要是不来了，我心里可就难受坏了。真到那步田地，我宁可不要牧师的儿子来——虽说我如今盼着他来，盼得欢欢喜喜。”

===Original===
“All right, Martha, then I shall come,” Cornelli promised. “We can easily be alone together in the kitchen, for I want to see you alone. I shall not come on Monday, for that is the day they arrive. On Tuesday, though, I’ll come. Then we’ll go together to the kitchen.”

Martha promised this and Cornelli went home in the same way as she had come. Not once did she run to the meadow to pick forget-me-nots or other flowers that were sparkling there.

When Monday came, she was wondering if a carriage would arrive with a proud city boy and a lady with a high feather hat, both of whom would look down on her with disdain. Cornelli settled down beside the garden fence, for from there she could conveniently survey the road. But she saw no carriage, though she watched through both the morning and the afternoon. She really was very glad, for she was quite sure that nobody had arrived. Next day when the time came for her to be free, she walked over to Martha’s little house.

“Oh, I am so glad that nobody has come. Now I can be alone with you and don’t have to go to the kitchen﻿—”

===Chinese===
“好吧，玛莎，那我就来。”科内莉答应了。“咱们很容易在厨房里单独待着，因为我只想单独见你。星期一我不来，因为那天他们到。不过星期二我就来。到时候咱们一块儿进厨房。”

玛莎应承下来，科内莉便照来时的样子回家去了。草地上勿忘我和别的花开得亮晶晶的，她一次也没有跑过去采。

到了星期一，她一直在心里嘀咕：会不会驶来一辆马车，车上坐着一个神气活现的城里男孩，还有一位戴着高高羽毛帽的太太，两个人都用瞧不起人的眼光打量她。科内莉在花园栅栏旁边守了下来，因为从那儿正好把大路望个一清二楚。可她从上午一直望到下午，连马车的影子也没见着。她心里真是高兴极了，因为她认定谁也没有来。第二天，一到她得空的时辰，她就往玛莎的小屋去了。

“哦，一个人也没来，我真高兴。这下我能单独跟你待着，不用进厨房了——”

===Original===
Cornelli had said these words on entering, but she suddenly stopped. A boy she had never seen sat at the table in the room and Martha was just clearing away the supper things. So he had come after all and had even heard what she had said. Oh, it was dreadful! But the boy was laughing.

Cornelli wanted to withdraw quickly, but the boy called out: “Please come in and let us get acquainted. Mrs. Martha has already told me about you. Just come in,” he continued, when he saw that Cornelli still hesitated. “If you want to be alone with Mrs. Wolf I can easily go to my own room.”

Cornelli felt that it was very nice of the boy not to resent her words and to be willing to give place to her. She therefore entered. Martha had already put a chair in readiness for her and greeted her heartily.

“I expected you, Cornelli,” she said. “Just sit down here a little with our guest. His name is Dino Halm and he already knows your name. I am sure you will have a good time together. I’ll go up in the meantime and if you need me you can find me in the room upstairs.”

Martha, thinking that the children could get acquainted better if they were left alone, had planned to unpack her new arrival’s things while they were together. She put his belongings neatly away in the wardrobe and the drawers in order to make him feel at home in his tidy little chamber.

===Chinese===
这些话是科内莉一进门就说出口的，可她猛地顿住了。屋里桌旁坐着一个她从没见过的男孩，玛莎正在收拾晚饭的杯盘。这么说，他到底还是来了，还偏偏听见了她说的话。唉，糟透了！可那男孩竟笑了起来。

科内莉想赶紧退出去，男孩却喊道：“请进来吧，咱们认识认识。玛莎太太已经跟我说过你了。快进来呀。”见科内莉还在犹豫，他又接着说：“你要是想同沃尔夫太太单独待着，我回自己屋去就得了。”

科内莉觉得这男孩真不错：不怪罪她方才的话，还情愿把地方让给她。于是她走了进去。玛莎已经给她备好了一把椅子，热热络络地招呼她。

“我就等着你来呢，科内莉。”她说。“先在这儿陪我们的客人坐一会儿。他叫迪诺·哈尔姆，也已经知道你的名字了。我担保你们会处得痛快的。我这就上楼去，你们要是找我，就到楼上那间屋里来。”

玛莎寻思，孩子们单独待着更容易混熟，便打算趁他们俩在一块儿的工夫，替新到的客人打开行李。她把他的东西整整齐齐地收进衣柜和抽屉里，好让他在那间整洁的小屋里住得像在自己家里一样自在。

===Original===
“Why did you think that we did not come?” asked Dino as soon as Martha had left the room and Cornelli was sitting beside him silently.

“Because I did not see the carriage,” she replied.

“The carriage? Well, I can believe you,” said Dino. “We walked more than an hour, in fact, nearly two, before we got here from the station. Do you just hop into a carriage when you go to the station?”

“Yes, I do; I always go there with Papa,” replied Cornelli.

“But where do the horses always come from?” Dino wanted to know.

“From our stable,” was the answer.

===Chinese===
“你为什么当我们没有来呢？”玛莎刚离开屋子、科内莉默默地挨着他坐下，迪诺就问了。

“因为我没看见马车呀。”她回答。

“马车？唔，这我信。”迪诺说。“从车站到这儿，我们走了不止一个钟头，说实在的，快两个钟头了。你们上车站，难道一抬腿就跳上马车吗？”

“是呀；我总是跟爸爸一块儿坐车去。”科内莉回答。

“可这些马都是打哪儿来的呢？”迪诺想知道。

“我们自家马厩里的。”回答就这么一句。

===Original===
“Have you your own carriage and two horses of your own, just to be able to drive about?” Dino questioned, full of astonishment.

“Yes, we have the two brown ones and six others to carry away the iron from the foundry.”

“Good gracious, eight horses!” Dino exclaimed. “You are lucky to be able to sit in a carriage with your father and drive around!”

“Can’t you do that?” asked Cornelli.

“Never in my life,” Dino replied in a voice full of conviction. “First of all, I do not have a father. Besides that, we do not own a stable and horses. How lucky you are! Have you anything else in the stable?”

“Oh yes, lots more. Six cows and a large gray stable cat,” Cornelli informed him. “Then there is an old nanny goat and a young snow white kid, about whose neck I tied a red ribbon. You are going to drink milk from our cow, did you know that?”

===Chinese===
“你们自己有一辆马车，还有两匹自家的马，单是为了能坐车出门逛逛？”迪诺问，惊讶极了。

“有，我们有两匹棕马，还有六匹，是替铸铁厂往外运铁用的。”

“好家伙，八匹马！”迪诺喊道。“能跟你爸爸坐在马车里到处跑，你可真有福气！”

“这你们坐不上吗？”科内莉问。

“这辈子也坐不上。”迪诺回答，语气笃定得很。“头一条，我就没有父亲。再说，我们也没有马厩和马。你多有福气啊！你们马厩里还有别的吗？”

“哦有，多着呢。六头奶牛，还有一只住惯了马厩的灰色大猫。”科内莉一样样报给他听。“再有一只老奶山羊和一只雪白的小羊羔，羊羔脖子上还系着我给它扎的红丝带呢。你往后喝的就是我们家奶牛的奶，你知道吗？”

===Original===
“Oh, I shall love to do that!” Dino exclaimed. “Do you think I’ll be allowed to go to the stable and look at the horses?”

“Certainly you will; Matthew will love to show them to you, and Martha will willingly let you go. If I only could go with you!” And Cornelli uttered a deep sigh.

“Well, I should think you certainly could do that, when the stable belongs to you. Who would hinder you, I’d like to know?” Dino said. “Do you know what we’ll do? We’ll hitch the little kid to a cart. Won’t that be lovely? It can pull you and I shall be the coachman. I once saw such a little carriage on a promenade in town.”

Cornelli had already had that thought herself, but she knew now that she could never again go to the stable. It was suddenly clear to her that she could not run about as before and that she could not be happy any more. The chief reason for it all was clear to her, the reason that prevented her from being carefree and bright as in the old times. She did not answer, but gave forth a profound sigh, profounder than the one she had uttered before.

“Why do you sigh, as if you had to carry a mountain about with you﻿—a load that keeps you from going forward? Why do you do it?” asked Dino.

“I can’t tell anyone. You couldn’t, either, if you had the trouble I have,” replied the little girl.

===Chinese===
“哦，那我可乐意了！”迪诺喊道。“你看能让我上马厩去看马吗？”

“当然能；马修巴不得领你去呢，玛莎也会痛痛快快放你去的。要是我也能跟你一块儿去就好了！”科内莉说着，深深叹了口气。

“嗐，马厩是你家的，这事你自然办得到。我倒要问问，谁拦着你不成？”迪诺说。“你知道咱们要干什么吗？咱们把小羊羔套上一辆小车。那多美呀！它拉着你，我来当车夫。有一回我在城里的林荫道上，就见过这么一辆小马车。”

这样的念头，科内莉自己早就有过，可她如今知道，自己再也不能去马厩了。她忽然全都清楚了：她再不能像从前那样跑来跑去，再也不能快活了。这中间最主要的缘故，她也明白了——就是那个缘故，害得她不能像旧日那样无忧无虑、欢欢喜喜。她没有答话，只发出一声深深的叹息，比先前那声叹得还深。

“你干吗叹气，倒像身上驮着一座山——那副重担压得你迈不开步似的？这是为什么呀？”迪诺问。

“我没法告诉任何人。你要是有我这样的难处，你也说不出口的。”小姑娘回答。

===Original===
“Oh, yes, I could. There is nothing in the world I couldn’t tell,” Dino asserted. “If you can’t confide in other people, you can always tell your mother, for she can always smooth everything out for you. Just go to her and tell her about it. That will relieve you and everything will come right.”

“Yes, and now I can say what you said to me before. You are lucky and much luckier than I am,” said Cornelli with a trembling voice. “I never can go to my mother because I have none. Now you see how well off I am! I am sure you would never exchange with me, would you?”

Dino looked quite frightened.

“I did not know that you had no mother,” he said, full of pity. In his mind he saw his own mother, the way she looked at him, so full of love that it always lightened his heart whenever anything troubled him. And poor Cornelli had to miss all that!

Even the stable with the horses, the large garden with all the fruit, about which Martha had told him so much, appeared to him now in a different light.

Full of decision he said: “No indeed, I would not change with you.”

===Chinese===
“哦，不，我说得出。天底下就没有我不能说的事。”迪诺一口咬定。“要是旁人那儿你信不过，你总可以告诉妈妈呀，她总有法子把一切都替你抚平了。这就去找她，把事情说给她听。说完了你就松快了，一切也都会好起来的。”

“是了，现在我可以把你先前对我说的话，回敬给你了。你有福气，比我福气大得多。”科内莉声音发颤地说。“我永远不能去找妈妈，因为我没有妈妈。这下你瞧见我多有福气了吧！我敢说，你决不肯跟我换，对不对？”

迪诺显出很吃惊的样子。

“我不知道你没有妈妈。”他说，满心怜惜。他心里浮现出自己的母亲，浮现出她望着自己的样子——那目光里的爱那么满，无论什么事压在心头，一想起它来，心里就轻快了。而可怜的科内莉，这一切都得缺着！

就连那有马儿的马厩、那结满果子的大园子——玛莎跟他讲过多少回的地方——此刻在他眼里，也换了一副光景。

他斩钉截铁地说：“不，说真的，我不跟你换。”

===Original===
But a great pity for the motherless child welled up in Dino’s heart and he longed to be her protector. He could understand now why Cornelli looked so strange; he had even noticed it as soon as he had seen her. There was no mother to fix everything the way it should be.

“We’ll try to be friends, Cornelli! But you must push your hair back from your forehead first of all; one can hardly see your eyes. Nobody wears hair like that. I don’t see how such long hair can stay there without blowing off. What on earth did you paste it on with?”

“With glue,” replied Cornelli.

“How nasty! Come, I’ll cut it all off, and then your eyes and your forehead will be clear. You can hardly see that way.”

Dino had seized the scissors that were lying beside Martha’s work basket, but Cornelli, struggling against him with both hands, fairly screamed: “Let it be. It has to be that way. Put the scissors away!”

===Chinese===
可是对这个没娘的孩子的一股深深的怜惜，从迪诺心里涌了上来，他渴望做她的保护人。这下他明白科内莉为什么显得那么古怪了；其实他一见面就觉察出来了。只因没有一个母亲，来把一切照着该有的样子安排妥帖。

“我们要做朋友，科内莉！不过你先得把头发从脑门上撩开，不然人家几乎看不见你的眼睛了。谁也没有这么梳头发的。我真不明白，这么长的头发，怎么就不叫风刮跑。你到底是用什么把它粘上去的？”

“用胶水。”科内莉回答。

“多别扭呀！来，我把它全铰掉，这样你的眼睛和脑门就敞亮了。老这么挡着，你自己看东西都费劲。”

迪诺已经抓起摆在玛莎针线筐旁边的剪刀，科内莉却双手跟他搏斗起来，尖声叫道：“别动它。它就得这样。把剪刀放下！”

===Original===
“I won’t hurt you. But don’t scream so loud!” said Dino quietly, putting down the scissors again. “I only wanted to do you a favor. If my two sisters, Agnes and Nika, could see you, they would laugh at you; they would not like the way you pasted on those locks.”

“I know that. But they do not need to see me at all,” said Cornelli crossly. “Nobody needs to see me. I know that nobody likes me, but I don’t care.”

With these words Cornelli ran away. Dino was terribly astonished and stood looking at the door through which Cornelli had disappeared without even a word of farewell.

When Martha again entered the little room and was looking at Cornelli’s empty chair, Dino said: “What a queer child she is. I never thought she would be so unfriendly.”

He related how they had passed the time together and how Cornelli had suddenly run off without even saying goodbye. He had not wanted to offend her.

===Chinese===
“我不会伤着你的。可你别嚷这么大声！”迪诺说着，又把剪刀放下了。“我不过是想帮你个忙。要是我的那两个姐妹，阿格妮丝和妮卡，见了你这副模样，准会笑话你；她们可不会喜欢你这么把一绺绺头发粘在脸上。”

“这我知道。可她们根本用不着看见我。”科内莉气冲冲地说。“谁都用不着看见我。我知道谁也不喜欢我，不过我不在乎。”

说完这话，科内莉就跑掉了。迪诺惊得目瞪口呆，站在那里望着那扇门——科内莉连一句道别的话都没有，就从那扇门里消失了。

玛莎再回到小屋里来，望着科内莉空着的那把椅子，迪诺说：“这孩子可真古怪。我万万没想到，她会这么不友好。”

他把他们俩在一块儿是怎么打发光阴的、科内莉又怎么连再见都没说一声就突然跑掉的事，原原本本讲了一遍。他并没有想得罪她的意思。

===Original===
Martha shook her head and said: “Cornelli never was that way before. I am so worried about her, for she is absolutely changed. You must not think that she is queer and runs away like that and suddenly gets cross. She never was that way at all; this is something new. If I only could hear her sing and laugh again as of old. I hoped that her old gaiety would come back with such a good playfellow as you are. Maybe it will; after all, this is only the first day of your acquaintance.

“I am sure Cornelli will not come back to me,” said Dino, still quite puzzled. “She ran away so full of anger.”
